JARVIS REPAIR PLAN (MASTER COPY)
================================
Written 2026-10-13 after the first live test. Since then Phases 0-5 have been built and committed, and
Phase 6 is built up to its live test (all code done; 6i / 6f are measure-only). See "CURRENT STATE"
just below for where things stand.
This file is the single source of truth. It is written so that a brand new chat can pick up any phase
with no other context. Read PART A first, then only the phase you are about to do.

TO ANY AI READING THIS FILE: FIRST CHECK THE TOOLS (added 2026-10-14)
  Before any work, check that each item below is present, then LIST for the user what you found: present,
  missing, or a different version. Do not install, update or configure anything missing without the user's
  approval (project CLAUDE.md). These are what the session that built Phases 2-4 had:

  Claude Code plugins (user scope; check ~/.claude/plugins/installed_plugins.json or /plugin):
    - context7          (anthropic-plugin-directory)  current library docs; the rule is in
                         ~/.claude/rules/context7.md. Also a user-scope MCP server "context7" in ~/.claude.json.
    - agent-skills 0.6.12 (addy-agent-skills)  structure for big tasks; agents code-reviewer,
                         security-auditor, test-engineer.
    - ponytail 5.2.0     (ponytail)  "lazy senior developer" mode, level full: smallest complete change,
                         and every reply ends with 1-2 lines on what was skipped and the risks.
    - tokenwise 0.2.0    (tokenwise)  model routing for sub-agents (block in the project CLAUDE.md); each
                         routed sub-agent adds one line to .tokenwise/log.ndjson (gitignored).
  User skills (~/.claude/skills): graphify (see below), context7-mcp.
  Python: the private venv %LOCALAPPDATA%\SpaceshipHUD\venv (3.12.10) and the global "py -3" (3.12.10).
  Lua check: the lupa package in a scratch folder (see "HANDOVER" below). Not installed globally.
  The account also has claude.ai connectors (Gmail, Google Calendar, Drive, Docs). This project does NOT use
  them. Never open the user's mail, calendar or files through them (privacy rule in A4).

  GRAPHIFY (a knowledge graph of this repo). USE IT.
    - Output: graphify-out\ in the repo root (gitignored, commit c5ff4a5). GRAPH_REPORT.md is the summary:
      god nodes, communities, surprising links. graph.html is the browsable map.
    - Interpreter: the path stored in graphify-out\.graphify_python (a uv tool install of graphifyy).
    - Use it to find cross-file links before reading code, e.g. /graphify query "how does alarm() stop"
      or /graphify path "alarm()" "wake_loop()". The graph is a map, not the truth: confirm in the code.
    - After a phase changes files, refresh it with /graphify update . (it re-reads only changed files).
    - .graphifyignore keeps Data\, logs, Backups\, model and sound files out of the graph. Keep it that way.
    - Known gap (2026-10-14): Docs\Spaceship-HUD-Guide.md was only partly re-read, so its nodes are from an
      older version. "graphify extract --force" rebuilds it in full (costs tokens; ask the user first).

BUGS TO WATCH OUT FOR WHEN IMPLEMENTING NEW CHANGES (added 2026-10-14; read before editing jarvis.py)
  Each one really happened in this project. Gathered here from the STATUS log, N13-N15, the HANDOVER and
  the redesign slices; the originals stay where they were and point here.

  W1. PYTHON CONTROL FLOW
    - A bare `return` inside `try:` runs `finally` and returns None. Code after the try/finally is NOT
      reached. (Slice D: exchange() lost its follow-up mode this way.) To hand a value out of such a
      function on every path, let it write into a dict the caller passes in, or return in every branch.
      Do not `return` inside `finally` (it swallows errors; Python 3.14 warns).
    - A nested function that assigns an outer variable needs `nonlocal` (or `global`), or the assignment
      makes a new local and the outer value never changes.
    - `a and b and "x" if c else "y"` is `(a and b and "x") if c else "y"`: the conditional binds weaker
      than `and`. Put brackets around the if/else part. (Slice D, the follow-up gate.)
    - A recursive call for "one more turn" grows the stack and hides the return value; turn() is a loop.

  W2. DATA THAT NEVER REACHES THE MODEL
    - A new way of picking prompt blocks must also be added to wants(), or the data is never in the prompt
      and the no-data reply never sees it. (Slice C: info_blocks() at 0.5-0.6 found the block, wants()
      at 0.6 did not.)
    - test_jarvis fakes j.context() to "" for most tests (about l.969). A test that needs HUD data must
      fake it again, or it silently tests the empty case.

  W3. THRESHOLDS AND WORD LISTS
    - Never pick a cut-off by feel: measure both sides (questions vs chat lines) and write the numbers in
      the plan (slice C: 0.46-0.84 vs up to 0.50).
    - keywords() checks stop-words BEFORE it cuts a trailing "s", so "likes" survives as "like" while
      "like" is a stop-word. Compare both sides through the same function (Checkpoint B, gist_ok()).
    - Whisper invents "Thank you." / "Thanks for watching." on noise (about 25 % of its hallucinations).
      A new path that reads transcripts must go through the hearing gate.
    - The model as FIRST reader is wrong too often (llama3.1:8b: 75 of 97, 14 questions became actions).
      Rules first, embedder second, model last; its output only through the said-words checks.

  W4. TESTS AND CHECK SCRIPTS
    - N13 (test clock): FIXED 2026-10-14 (AUDIT 2). The alarm tests pin the clock to 3 AM, so no TZ is
      needed. A new test that reads the clock must pin it the same way (j.time.time = lambda: real_time()
      + at_3am, then restore it).
    - N14 (check_route.py): a route with a due time can show as "different" while both sides print the
      same tuple. Treat that one line as noise.
    - Tests that assert a log line ("route: ...") break when the route line format changes; update them
      on purpose, and read why (the ai-reread cancel test ends before the lane is set).
    - A fake chat_stream must accept every keyword the real one takes (temperature since slice C).
    - Debug prints go only into the scratch copy. Copy clean files over it before the next run.
    - Importing jarvis.py writes Data\jarvis.txt: run tests in the scratchpad only, never in the repo.

  W5. EDITING THE FILES
    - The Bash tool turns `\\b`, `\\w` in a heredoc with Python into real backspace / control characters.
      Use the Edit tool or a script file. Then check: grep -cP '[\x00-\x08\x0b\x0c\x0e-\x1f]' jarvis.py
      test_jarvis.py  must print 0 for each.
    - Script edits: assert each old text occurs exactly once, open with newline="" to keep line endings.
    - "LF will be replaced by CRLF" from git is a warning only; do not convert files.
    - N15: a Control Center row draws at most 4 buttons; a 5th is silently not drawn. New buttons: new row.
    - Lua is 5.1 (Rainmeter); there is no stand-alone Lua, so new rows are unverified until a live run.

  W6. GIT AND THE LIVE COPY
    - Never git checkout / restore / stash to undo: it also wipes earlier uncommitted work. Revert by hand
      from the diff, or git revert after a commit.
    - Stage by name and read the staged list. Never commit Data\, logs, weights, keys.
    - The live skin is a COPY: a fix is not live until it is copied (robocopy backup first, user's word).

  - 2026-10-14: T5 (M4a-2) BUILT, NOT committed, NOT live. jarvis.py:
    - Save phrases: "remember (that)", plus "note that / don't forget that / keep in mind that" + at least two
      words ("note that down" stays chat).
    - fact_save(): an older fact sharing half or more of the new one's keywords asks "You told me '...'.
      Replace it with this? Confirm with yes." Yes replaces it (ACTIONS replace_fact). CHANGED FROM THE SPEC
      on purpose: anything but a yes still saves the new fact and says "I kept the old one too.", because you
      asked for the save, the yes only guards the old fact, and a wrong "same thing" guess must not lose
      what you said. "undo that" removes the new fact.
    - Forget: more than one match reads them all back, "Forget N things: ...? Confirm with yes."
      (ACTIONS forget_facts). One match works as before.
    - "What do you know about X": your saved facts by code, "You told me: ...". X = me / myself -> all.
      Nothing saved about X -> the model answers (so "what do you know about black holes" still works).
    - run_action logs a target only for close / open / volume / media (an allow-list; same lines as before).
    - Not built (not in T5's acceptance): 6d's "a close question answered by code with the stored words".
    Checks: test_jarvis OK (py -3, venv, TZ=ABC+12); route check 65 phrases, only the time-stamp line.
    NEXT: T6 (M4a-3, offer to remember).
  - 2026-10-14: T6 (M4a-3) BUILT, NOT committed, NOT live. jarvis.py:
    - Clear requests save at once, more wordings: "oh / also / and remember ...", "remember, ...",
      "I want (need) you to remember that ...", and a trailing "..., remember that / don't forget that /
      keep that in mind". "remember that" alone, a question, "I can't remember that", "do you remember that
      film" and "remember to call mum" do not save.
    - Offer: after a chat answer, a lasting fact about you in your words (fixed patterns: my sister's name
      is, my favourite X is, I'm allergic to, I work at, I live in, I'm vegetarian, I study, I have a X
      called ...) gets one "Want me to remember that?". Only a yes (or "remember that" / "save it") in the
      next turn, within 30 s + the follow-up wait, saves it. Anything else drops the offer and goes on as
      usual. Never offered: questions, numbers of 3+ digits, passwords/PINs/codes/cards/accounts/bank,
      anything SECRET matches, facts already saved. JarvisLearn=off (or 0) turns offers off (default: ask).
      The offered fact is held in memory only and never logged.
    - Shortcut: fixed patterns, not the model, so unusual wording is missed (comment in code).
    Checks: test_jarvis OK (py -3, venv, TZ=ABC+12); route check 65 phrases, only the time-stamp line.
    NEXT: T7 (M4b, recent actions).
  - 2026-10-14: T7 (M4b), T8 (M3a), T9 (M3b) BUILT, NOT committed, NOT live. jarvis.py:
    - T7: the last 20 actions that really ran this session (time, what, failed or not), held in memory only.
      Logged by code where they run: PC actions (opened X, closed X, locked, sleep, volume, media ...), saved /
      forgotten / replaced facts, timers, alarms, reminders (with their time), cancel, move, snooze, undo.
      They go into the prompt only for questions about him ("what did you do", "did you set a timer", "have
      you opened ...") with "only these ran; never claim anything else". None: "You ran nothing in this session."
    - T8: once a day, when you have been quiet 10 min, JARVIS is idle and the HUD is FULL (not LITE/OFF), topics
      unused 7 days (or the oldest past 100) go to the model, which returns up to 3 short facts. Code checks each:
      120 characters or fewer, every content word, name and number must be in that talk. Passed facts go to
      Data\jarvis_gist.txt ("date|fact"), the topic is removed. None passed: the topic stays, tried again the
      next day, removed after the 3rd failed try. A turn starting or the HUD leaving FULL stops it between topics.
      The gist comes back in recall ("- earlier: ...") and in "what do you remember about X" ("From before: ...").
      Only counts are logged.
    - T9: once a month, only over 200 gist lines: among the oldest 100, lines sharing 2+ keywords are merged into
      fewer by the model under the same check (about 30 at most a month). jarvis_memory.txt (your facts) is never
      touched.
    - Deviations from the spec (better, I'm sure): model down does NOT count a try (an off PC would otherwise
      delete topics unread); the topic file cap went 100 -> 200 (compaction brings it back to 100 daily); the
      check ignores stop-words, so "likes" passes when you said "like".
    - Not built: forgetting a gist line by voice ("forget what you know about X" only clears facts and topics).
    Checks: test_jarvis OK (py -3, venv, TZ=ABC+12); route check 65 phrases, 0 routed differently.
    NEXT: Checkpoint B (commit on your word), then T11 (6j).
  - 2026-10-14: COMMITTED c33d37f on the user's word (Checkpoint B: T4-T9, voice to Phase 10). Not pushed,
    not live. Two bugs the T8 tests caught, fixed before the commit: (1) gist_ok() rejected "The user likes X"
    when you had said "I like X": keywords() checks stop-words BEFORE it cuts a trailing "s", so "likes" stays
    as "like" while "like" itself is a stop-word. Fixed only in gist_ok() (keywords(fact) - TALK_STOP);
    keywords() itself is left as it is, because topic and fact matching depend on it (known quirk, harmless
    there: both sides go through the same function). (2) compact() wrote "tries": 0 onto every topic; now
    only onto the topics it actually tried.
  - 2026-10-14: T11 (6j, route + timing log) BUILT, NOT committed, NOT live. jarvis.py turn(): a small "way"
    dict is filled where the path is decided and logged ONCE in finally:
      route: <kind>[ <action>] via rule | ai-reread | model, total <s>s
    total = seconds from the end of your speech to the end of the turn (for a chat turn: until he finished
    speaking). kind / action are names from the code only (the action is printed only if it is a plain
    lower-case word), never what you said or a target. Paths: "rule" = route() or a fixed check decided it,
    "ai-reread" = interpret() re-read a missed command and its reading was used, "model" = the chat model
    answered (chat, notes, apps, guard, brief). Extra kinds: "new_topic" ("new topic" alone), "log" (show /
    close the log), "memory save" (a yes to "Want me to remember that?"). Turns with no words (silence, not
    heard, ended by you on a follow-up) log no route line. The existing "timing:" lines stay as they were.
    Action names to expect: e.g. a timer / alarm / reminder is "remind set", the volume question "level".
    Tests: a chat, a level question, a timer, "new topic", "show me what you've said" each log exactly one
    line with the right path and none of the question's words; the ai-reread test logs "act volume via
    ai-reread". Checks: test_jarvis OK (py -3, venv, TZ=ABC+12); route check 65 phrases, only the N14
    time-stamp line (old and new print the same tuple). Rollback: before_t11_jarvis.py / before_t11_test.py
    in the 28aef137 scratchpad, or reverse the diff.
    PHASE 6 CODE IS NOW COMPLETE. NEXT: commit on the user's word -> copy to live on the user's word -> the
    PHASE 6 LIVE TEST CHECKLIST -> 6i / 6f measure.
  - 2026-10-14: 6j committed as aa3a48d. Then a ponytail audit of jarvis.py (report only) found 4 things,
    fixed and committed as b82b3b5 (jarvis.py +29/-6, test_jarvis.py +16):
      1. "forget X" matched parts of words ("forget car" deleted "my friend Oscar ...") and a single forget
         could not be undone. Now holds_words() matches whole words (route() and memory_reply), and
         can_undo("forget", gone) + an undo_last "forget" branch put the facts back ("Put back: ...").
      2. chroma_index: one note it could not open (locked / deleted mid-sync) set failed=True and turned
         smart search off until restart. Now that note is skipped and retried on the next pass.
      3. Tests for 1 and 2.
      4. vec() cache limit 500 -> 1000 (warm-up: 23 examples + 200 topics + 50 facts + gist lines, and
         the gist has no hard cap, so it could pass 500 and empty itself). About 12 KB an entry.
    Checks: py_compile OK; test_jarvis OK under TZ=ABC-8 with py -3 and venv. TZ=ABC+12 FAILED at that
    hour on HEAD too (see N13 update), so not a regression. Not live. Rollback: git revert b82b3b5.
    Not done in the audit: most of the Lua, ShipCore.cs, tools\.

      heredocs with Python inside can fail ("unexpected EOF"); a script file always works.

A1. HOW WE WORK (agreed with the user)
  - One phase at a time. Inside a phase, one fix at a time.
  - After each phase the user tests it live and says whether the issue is gone. Only then the next phase.
  - Each phase starts with a failing test, then the fix, then the checks in PART E.
  - Nothing is committed, pushed, merged or copied to the live skin without the user's explicit word.
  - To start a phase in a new chat, say: "Read JARVIS-Repair-Plan.txt and do Phase N."

A4. RULES THAT ALWAYS HOLD (from the user and the project CLAUDE.md)
  Safety
    - "Dont break anything." Smallest change that fully solves the task. No rewrite, no framework change.
    - Preserve uncommitted work. No destructive git. Never "git add ." without reading the staged list.
    - No commit, push, merge, release or deploy without explicit approval.
    - Before each significant change: goal, scope, non-goals, acceptance, validation, risks, rollback.
  Actions
    - The model may propose. Plain code owns authorization, confirmation, running, exit codes and the log.
    - OS actions need an exact confirmation tied to one pending action, and it expires.
    - No arbitrary shell execution. Only fixed entries in ACTIONS can run.
    - Tests never really mute audio, close apps, connect devices or shut the machine down. Fakes only.
    - No destructive or disruptive action on the live Windows session during testing.
  Privacy
    - Never read or print: Data\, the log files, calendar, mail, the notes vault, the reminders and memory
      files, the ChromaDB index, the personal lines of Settings.inc.
    - New log lines hold numbers and states only. Never the user's words.
    - Never commit keys, tokens, notes, model weights, recordings, logs, local databases or caches.
  Packages and models
    - No new package, model or download before showing the smallest experiment and its disk and memory cost.
    - Never change the global site-packages. Only the private venv, and only with approval.
    - Never pick "the first model in the list". Compare and choose by result.
    - The Windows voice stays as a fallback. One microphone session at a time, always.
  Reporting
    - Report the exact commands run and what they printed. Never say a test passed unless it ran.
    - Anything that needs a real microphone, speaker, GPU or live session is "unverified" until the user
      asks for a live run.
  Tools
    - Context7 for library documentation. No sub-agents unless the user asks. Planning stays on Opus.
    - Graphify (graphify-out\) for cross-file links; refresh it after a phase. See the TOOLS block at the top.

  14. The memory function needs to be improved or relooked.                                     [Phase 6]
  15. It can think Jarvis is speaking after I stop it.                                          [Phase 4]
  16. The snooze function doesn't work.                                               [Phase 1, Phase 5]
  17. The follow up should only work if I respond within the next 3 seconds, or if I say
      "Jarvis" and then something within 10 seconds after.                                      [Phase 8]
  18. I want the responses to sound more human.                              [Phase 6, speed in Phase 2]
  19. It keeps constantly falling back to my CPU, even if my GPU is on. Turning on JARVIS should
      turn on my GPU too.                                                                       [Phase 2]
  20. Add additional settings he can change, including microphone settings and Bluetooth settings
      (if I say "connect to ___" I want it to try and connect to whatever he can).   [Phase 3, Phase 7]
  21. Give him system logs and Task Manager information, so he can see which processes are
      overloading the system, to the point where I can just ask him what the issue is.          [Phase 9]
  22. Access to more settings that are easy to integrate, like airplane mode and more.          [Phase 7]
  Added on 2026-10-13 from the recommendations the user accepted:
  23. A stop key on the keyboard, ONLY for interrupting him (it never wakes him).               [Phase 4]
  24. Time, date, sums and unit conversions answered by code, at once.                          [Phase 1]
  25. "Undo that" and "what did you hear?"                                                      [Phase 1]
  26. Turn other sound down while he listens.                                                   [Phase 3]
  Added on 2026-10-14 from the user's use of the Phase 1-4 build:
  27. The GPU usage can spike to 100% whenever Jarvis is clicked on (started), starts thinking,
      or starts speaking.                                                                       [Phase 6]
  28. He can lie about important things when trying to be playful, and lied about the GPU spikes
      (talked about running simulations he never ran). A personality issue.                     [Phase 6]
  REJECTED by the user: showing what he heard on the hologram. Do not build it. Mishearing is to be
  handled by autocorrect and by ignoring slight differences (Phase 1a and 3b).

  Facts from the user's test log (safe, no personal content):
    - Turns were started by a click, never by the wake word.
    - The name was heard as "Travis" and "droves".
    - Replies took 15 to 28 seconds.
    - "Set an alarm for in one minute" went to the chat model, which answered that the alarm was set.
      Nothing was set. A later "do I have an alarm set" was answered "no".
    - The model called itself the film character and named a film creator.
    - Problem lines: speech recognition not on the GPU (JarvisWhisperDevice=cpu); the voice not on the GPU
      (no GPU build of onnxruntime); voice failed with: module 'numpy' has no attribute 'matrix'.


========================================================================================================
PART C   WHAT WAS FOUND
========================================================================================================

C1. THE FIVE ROOT CAUSES
  1. Commands fall through to the chat model, and the chat model lies.
     - route() strips only the exact name "Jarvis". A misheard "Travis, ..." keeps the name in the command,
       and every command pattern is anchored on the start, so volume, mute, media, open and timer all miss.
     - reminder_cmd() knows only the words "timer" and "reminder". "Alarm" is unknown.
     - Anything unmatched goes to chat. Nothing in SYSTEM tells the model it cannot perform actions.
     - The volume pattern (about l.1394) needs digits and exact wording.
     Explains issues 8, 13, part of 10, and most likely 16 (there never was an alarm to snooze).
  2. Nothing listens while he speaks or rings.
     - wake_loop() drops the microphone whenever `busy` is held (l.2225).
     - play_samples() only checks the cancel flag, and only a click sets it.
     - alarm() listens only in the gap after the ding and the spoken line.
     Explains issues 4, 7, 9, part of 16.
  3. Every prompt carries everything.
     - system_prompt() always adds the profile (up to 2000-4000 characters), all remembered facts (up to
       2000 characters), context() (mail count, up to 6 calendar events, open apps, network, up to 12
       to-dos) and sensors(). SYSTEM says "use it when relevant". turn() always sends the last 8 messages
       and forgets only after 10 minutes.
     Explains issues 5, 11, part of 14, part of 3.
  4. Hearing runs on the weakest settings.
     - base.en, beam 1, no VAD filter, no hint that the name exists, and in the live skin: on the CPU.
     Explains issue 10 and part of 12.
  5. The GPU is not used although it works (see C2).
     Explains issue 19 and a large part of the 15-28 second replies.

C2. THE GPU: MEASURED ON 2026-10-13 (read-only commands, nothing changed)
  Machine:   Lenovo 83Q6, hybrid laptop. Two graphics cards:
               AMD Radeon 880M (built in)
               NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5070 Laptop GPU, 12 GB, driver 592.25 (2.4 GB in use at the time)
  On mains power at the time of the check.

  What runs where, and why:
    Part                  Now      Why
    --------------------  -------  ------------------------------------------------------------------
    Chat model (Ollama)   GPU      "ollama ps" showed llama3.2:3b at 100% GPU. Ollama starts with
                                   Windows (Startup folder). The user variables that hide the Radeon
                                   card (ROCR_VISIBLE_DEVICES, HIP_VISIBLE_DEVICES) are already set.
    Speech recognition    CPU      The LIVE Settings.inc says JarvisWhisperDevice=cpu. The repo default
                                   is cuda. That one setting forces the CPU. The GPU libraries (cuBLAS,
                                   cuDNN 9, CUDA 12) are ALREADY in the venv, and a test in the venv
                                   loaded base.en on the RTX and ran it in 4.4 seconds. No download needed.
    Voice (Kokoro)        CPU      By choice in the code (l.719-723). The installed onnxruntime is the
                                   CPU build. Its GPU build 1.31 wants CUDA 13 while Whisper uses
                                   CUDA 12, and both ship a file named cudnn64_9.dll. So the warning
                                   "CPU in use - the voice" appears on EVERY start. It is not a fallback.

  Sticky fallbacks in the code (the real "keeps falling back"):
    - whisper() loads once. If the card was asleep, off or busy at that moment, it loads on the CPU and
      stays there until the idle unload (10 minutes without a turn).
    - transcribe() switches to the CPU for good on any error that mentions cuda, cublas or cudnn.
    - A chat model that Ollama loaded while the card was off stays off the card until it is unloaded.
    - ollama_on_cpu() warns but does nothing about it.

  Can JARVIS turn the GPU on?
    - In hybrid mode: yes, and it already happens by itself. The RTX sleeps when idle and the first CUDA
      call wakes it. Loading Whisper on the card at start is that call.
    - If Lenovo's "integrated graphics only" mode is on, Windows no longer sees the card at all. A normal
      program cannot switch that back. It may be scriptable through Lenovo's own interface (unverified).
    - The Windows "graphics preference" list has an entry for Rainmeter.exe only. That list steers DirectX
      programs. CUDA talks to the NVIDIA driver directly, so entries for Python or Ollama would not help.

C3. THE VOICE CRASH (issue 2)
  - Not an old numpy. The venv has numpy 2.5.4, which has numpy.matrix. Only joblib (numpy_pickle.py:378)
    refers to it.
  - Likely cause, NOT yet reproduced: main() starts five threads at once (l.2323-2328) and four of them
    import numpy, onnxruntime, faster-whisper, openwakeword and the notes index at the same moment. One
    thread can see a half-loaded numpy.
  - Second fault, certain: synth_audio() marks the voice broken after ONE failure and never tries again
    until restart, so one bad moment means the Windows voice for the whole session.

C4. OTHER FINDINGS
  - Wake word: openWakeWord "hey_jarvis", onnx, threshold JarvisWakeSensitivity (0.5), no speech check.
    It runs only when JarvisWakeWord=1, JarvisEnabled=1 and the HUD tier is below 3. The wake thread is
    one of the five that start together, so the same start-up race could kill it. Whether "wake word
    ready" is in the live log is unknown (the log was not read).
  - Follow-up: after a spoken chat answer, 0.3 s pause, waits about 6 s, at most 3 in a row.
  - Alarm: RINGS=10; each ring is ding, spoken line, then up to about 6 s of hear() on the CPU.
  - Tone: default JarvisPersonality is "playful".
  - Sounds: chime() uses winsound (full volume, no control). boot.lua plays startup.wav. The words
    "Jarvis online" are spoken only in Setup-Jarvis.ps1 (l.99, l.136) and check_voice.py (l.19). They are
    not in the daemon, so the sound is probably recorded inside startup.wav or chime.wav. Not confirmed.
  - "Thinks he is still speaking after I stop him" (issue 15): not pinned down. Candidates: the audio
    stream drains its buffer instead of aborting; the Windows-voice helper process keeps running (up to
    60 s); the HUD state file is written late; the follow-up microphone hears leftover sound.
  - Installed in the venv: numpy 2.5.4, kokoro-onnx 0.6.1, onnxruntime 1.31.0 (CPU), faster-whisper 1.2.1,
    ctranslate2 4.8.2, openwakeword 0.6.0, sounddevice 0.5.6, scipy 1.18.1, chromadb 1.5.9,
    nvidia-cublas-cu12 12.9.2.10, nvidia-cudnn-cu12 9.27.0.42.
  - Windows reports 8 Bluetooth-class devices and 2 working audio endpoints (counts only).

Order: what breaks trust first, then what every later phase depends on, then comfort.

   Phase 0   Measure (read-only)

   Phase 2   GPU first                                       issues 19, 18 (speed)
   Phase 3   Hearing you correctly                           issues 10, 12, 20 (microphone choice), 26
   Phase 4   Interrupting him                                issues 4, 7, 15, 23
   Phase 5   Alarms that behave like alarms                  issues 8, 9, 16
   Phase 6   Relevance, memory, sounding human               issues 5, 11, 14, 18, 27
   Phase 7   More settings by voice                          issues 13, 20, 22

   Phase 10  Personality and voice (tone, honesty, speech)   issues 3, 28
   Phase 10B Live-test fixes + usability (before Phase 11)   the 2026-10-17/20 live test complaints, verified Gemini items
   Phase 8 is small and independent. It can be done at any point for a quick win.
