PHASE 0   MEASURE FIRST  (read-only, about an hour)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Fixes: nothing yet. It turns four guesses into facts.

  Question                               How (nothing personal is read)
  -------------------------------------  ----------------------------------------------------------------
  Is the voice crash a start-up race?    A scratch script outside the repo starts four threads that import
                                         numpy, onnxruntime, faster_whisper, openwakeword and sklearn at
                                         once. 30 runs in the venv. Count the AttributeErrors.
  Does the wake word thread start?       Count lines in the log that match FIXED status texts only:
                                         "wake word ready", "wake word:", "problem:". Counts, no content.
                                         Needs the user's OK because it opens the log.
  Where do the 15-28 seconds go?         Add one timing line per turn (milliseconds only): heard,
                                         transcribed, first model word, first sound.
  Where is "Jarvis online" spoken?       Length in seconds of startup.wav and chime.wav. The user confirms
                                         by ear which one says it.
  Why is the live setting "cpu"?         Ask the user. The Control Center row cycles AUTO / CPU / CUDA
                                         (control.lua l.551-555), so one click can have set it.
  Is the laptop in hybrid mode?          gpu() already answers it: True means the card is attached.

Output: a short table of numbers at the top of the chat. Phases 1, 2 and 3 use it.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PHASE 1   HE TELLS THE TRUTH AND COMMANDS REALLY RUN
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Fixes:  issue 2  (the voice broke)
        issue 13 (doesn't actually change settings like volume)
        issue 8  (doesn't remember the alarms it set: they were never set)
        issue 10 (the "Travis" half of autocorrect)
        issue 16 (snooze: there was no alarm to snooze)
        issue 24 (time, date, sums and conversions answered by code)
        issue 25 ("undo that" and "what did you hear?")
Why first: a helper that says "done" when nothing happened cannot be trusted with anything else.
Size: about 185 lines in jarvis.py plus tests. 1f and 1g can be done as a second step after 1a to 1e.

1a. Name normalisation (about 10 lines)
  - One table of what Whisper writes for the name: jarvis, travis, jervis, javis, jarvus, service, droves,
    drovis.
  - One regex removes an optional "hey / hi / ok / okay" plus one of these, ONLY as the first words of the
    sentence. "Play Travis Scott" stays untouched.
  - It runs before route(), so every anchored command works again.
  - New setting JarvisNameAliases (comma list) to add more. Logged as a count only.

1b. Alarm words (about 15 lines, in reminder_cmd)
  - "alarm" and "wake me up" become a reminder with a clock time or a length:
    "set an alarm for in one minute", "alarm at 7 am", "wake me up at 6:30",
    "do I have an alarm set", "cancel my alarm".
  - Stored with kind "alarm" in the existing file (due|created|kind|label). No format change. Older code
    reads an unknown kind as a plain reminder that rings once.

1c. Volume phrases (about 25 lines, near l.1394)
  - Number words through the existing _num(): "set the volume to thirty".
  - "a bit / a little / slightly" = 10 steps. "louder / quieter", "turn it up / down", "max", "half",
    "set it to 30", "volume 30".
  - The reply keeps coming from done_line(): the value Windows reports, never the one asked for.
  - More settings (microphone, Bluetooth, brightness and others) are Phase 7.

1d. The model may never claim an action (about 25 lines)
  Three layers, strongest first:
  1. Near-miss catch, in code. A sentence that has an action verb (set, turn, change, open, close, mute,
     cancel, connect, snooze) AND an action noun (volume, alarm, timer, reminder, app, bluetooth,
     microphone, brightness) that no parser matched does NOT go to the model. Fixed reply:
     "I heard a command but couldn't work it out. Try: set a timer for one minute."
     Sentences that start with how, why, what, when, who, can you explain stay chat.
  2. Claim check, in code. A chat answer that says an action was done ("alarm set", "I've turned",
     "volume is now", "I have set", "reminder added") is replaced by a fixed line, because no action can
     run on the chat path.
  3. One line in SYSTEM: "You cannot set alarms, change settings or open apps. Never say you did."
  Why three: research on small models says prompt rules alone do not hold. Home Assistant tries fixed
  commands first and skips the model when one matches. The agent literature calls the fault an
  "execution hallucination" and says success must be reported from the real result only.

1e. The voice crash (about 12 lines)
  - Import numpy, onnxruntime, faster_whisper, openwakeword and sounddevice ONCE on the main thread in
    main(), before any thread starts. (Only if Phase 0 confirms the race. It is harmless either way.)
  - synth_audio(): one retry at once, then try again after 60 seconds, instead of "broken until restart".
    Log the error type only.
  - The Windows voice stays as the fallback.

1f. Answers by code: time, date, sums, conversions (issue 24; about 60 lines, standard library only)
  Today these go to the chat model: 15 to 28 seconds, and a 3B model gets sums wrong.
  route() gets a new kind "fact", checked AFTER the action and reminder parsers and before chat:
    Asked                                         Answered from
    --------------------------------------------  ------------------------------------------------------
    "what time is it", "what's the date",         the PC clock
    "what day is it", "what day is the 3rd"
    "how many days until Friday / until           date arithmetic (datetime)
    December 25th", "what's the date in 2 weeks"
    "what's 12 times 9", "15 percent of 240",     a small calculator: numbers and + - x / percent, power
    "240 divided by 7", "square root of 81"       and square root ONLY. It parses the words itself and
                                                  never runs spoken text as code. Limits on size.
    "how many km in 26 miles", "5 feet 10 in      one fixed table of factors: length, weight, volume,
    centimetres", "100 fahrenheit in celsius",    temperature, speed, area, time, data sizes
    "200 grams in ounces", "2 hours in seconds"
  - Number words go through the existing _num() ("twelve times nine").
  - The answer is rounded for the ear: "About 41.8 kilometres." "108."
  - Anything it cannot parse fully falls through to chat exactly as today. No near-miss catch here.
  - Not built: currency (needs the internet), time zones of other cities, anything with units mixed in
    a sum ("3 km plus 2 miles").
  - check_route.py will show "what time is it" changing route. That is on purpose; list it as expected.

1g. "Undo that" and "what did you hear?" (issue 25; about 35 lines)
  - run_action() keeps ONE record of the last action that changed something, with the value before it.
    It expires after 2 minutes. "Undo that", "undo", "put it back", "cancel that" reverse it:
      Last action                       Undo does
      --------------------------------  ---------------------------------------------------------
      volume set or stepped             the volume from before
      mute / unmute                     the other one
      timer, reminder or alarm set      removes that one item
      a fact saved to memory            removes that fact
      power mode changed (7d)           the mode from before
      microphone level, brightness (7)  the value from before
    Cannot be undone, and he says so: shut down, restart, sleep, lock, close an app, a media key,
    an emptied recycle bin, a to-do line (delete it in Obsidian), a fact that was forgotten.
  - He reports the real result as always: "Volume back at 45 percent."
  - "What did you hear?" repeats the last sentence as transcribed. "Say that again" / "repeat that"
    repeats his last answer. Both are kept in memory only, for the session, and never logged.
  - Nothing to undo, or expired: "There's nothing to undo."

Tests (fakes, in test_jarvis.py)
  - 1f: 25 phrases give the right spoken answer with the fake model never called; "what's 2 to the
    power of 9999999" is refused, not computed; text that is not numbers and operators reaches chat.
  - 1g: volume 45 -> 30 -> undo gives ("set", 45); undo twice says nothing to undo; undo after the
    fake 2 minutes says nothing to undo; undo after a cancelled "shut down" does nothing.
  - 20 misheard-name sentences route exactly like the clean ones.
  - "play Travis Scott" keeps the name.
  - Alarm: set by length, set by clock, list, cancel.
  - 15 volume phrases give the right ("set", n) or ("step", n).
  - A near-miss sentence gives the fixed line and the fake model is never called.
  - A fake model answer "Alarm set for 5 pm" is replaced.
  - A fake Kokoro that fails once works on the retry and is not marked broken.
  - check_route.py: no phrase that worked before changes its route.

Your live test after Phase 1
  1. Click, say "Travis, set the volume to thirty."       -> volume changes, he says "Volume at 30 percent."
  2. "Turn it down a bit."                                -> 20 percent.
  3. "Set an alarm for one minute."                       -> a real alarm rings a minute later.
  4. "Do I have an alarm set?" (before it rings)          -> he names it.
  5. "Change the screen colour to blue." (not a feature)  -> he says he couldn't work it out. No false "done".
  6. Restart JARVIS five times.                           -> the Kokoro voice every time, no Windows voice.
  7. "What time is it?", "what's 15 percent of 240?", "how many kilometres is 26 miles?"
                                                          -> each answered within a second or two.
  8. "Set the volume to 30", then "undo that."            -> back to the volume from before.
  9. "What did you hear?"                                 -> he repeats your last sentence.

Risk: the near-miss catch could take a real question. The question-word rule limits it.
Rollback: git checkout of the two files before a commit, git revert after. No data format changed.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PHASE 2   GPU FIRST
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Fixes:  issue 19 (keeps falling back to the CPU even when the GPU is on; JARVIS should turn the GPU on)
        issue 18 (part: the 15-28 second replies are the biggest reason he does not feel human)
Why second: it is mostly one setting and about 40 lines, it makes every turn faster, and Phase 3 needs
the GPU to afford better hearing.
Read C2 first. The short version: Ollama is already on the RTX. Speech recognition is on the CPU because
the live setting says so. The voice is on the CPU by design.

2a. Put speech recognition on the RTX (no download)
  - ANSWERED by the user on 2026-10-13: the "cpu" value was NOT set on purpose. Move it to the GPU.
  - The user sets WHISPER DEVICE to AUTO in the Control Center (the JARVIS section; it cycles
    AUTO / CPU / CUDA). This is a click in the live skin, done by the user. I do not edit the live skin.
  - Proven in the venv on 2026-10-13: base.en loads and runs on the RTX in 4.4 seconds.

2b. Stop the sticky fallbacks (about 25 lines)
  - A "GPU watch" in the main loop's 2-second heartbeat, only between turns (never while `busy`):
    if gpu() is True, the setting allows it, and Whisper is on the CPU, load it again on the card.
  - transcribe(): after a GPU error, use the CPU for THAT sentence only, and let the GPU watch try the
    card again later. At most one reload a minute, so a broken card cannot cause a loop.
  - The chat model: when ollama_on_cpu() is True and gpu() is True, unload it (keep_alive 0) and warm it
    again, once.
  - Each move is logged with a number and a state only: "whisper moved to GPU (1.9 s)".

2c. "Turning on JARVIS turns on the GPU"
  - At start, wake the card on purpose: the Whisper preload on the card is that call. On a hybrid laptop
    the RTX powers up by itself for it.
  - If the card is not attached at all (gpu() False), the hologram says WHY in plain words:
    "RTX is off - switch the laptop to hybrid graphics" instead of the general "CPU in use" line. When the
    card comes back, the GPU watch (2b) moves everything over without a restart.
  - New setting JarvisGPU = prefer (default) | require.
      prefer:  use the CPU when the card is away, and say so once.
      require: never run a turn on the CPU. He says "My graphics card is off" and stops.
  - Switching Lenovo's graphics mode from code: NOT planned. It changes a hardware mode and may need a
    restart. Optional experiment, only on the user's word: a read-only look at whether this model exposes
    the switch. Until then the user switches it in the Lenovo software.

2d. The voice warning (2 lines now, the real fix in 2e)
  - The voice on the CPU is the designed state, so it stops being a "problem" line on every start. It
    becomes one plain log line. A real fallback (Whisper or the chat model off the card) stays a warning.

2e. The voice on the GPU (experiment, needs approval before anything is installed)
  Blocker: onnxruntime-gpu 1.31 wants CUDA 13, Whisper uses CUDA 12, both ship cudnn64_9.dll.
  Three ways, tried in a COPY of the venv, never the real one:
    A. onnxruntime-directml instead of onnxruntime. It runs on the graphics card through DirectX, needs no
       CUDA at all, so it cannot clash with Whisper. Small download. To check: that the int8 Kokoro model
       runs on it and is faster, and that it picks the RTX, not the Radeon.
    B. An onnxruntime-gpu version built for CUDA 12, so both use the same libraries.
    C. Leave the voice on the CPU (today). Measured earlier: capped threads cost about 0.6 s a sentence.
  Decision by measurement: seconds to first sound for the same three sentences, and VRAM used.
  Shown before any install: package name, version, size on disk, how to go back.
  RESULT (2026-10-14, measured with Whisper loaded on the card in the same process):
    A. directml 1.24.4: fails on both adapters (ConvTranspose, 80070057). Dropped.
    B. onnxruntime-gpu 1.23.2 + nvidia-cuda-runtime-cu12 + nvidia-cufft-cu12 (requirements-gpu.txt).
       int8 model on CUDA: 3.1 s first sentence (slower than the CPU). The full model kokoro-v1.0.onnx
       (325 MB): 0.24-0.5 s a sentence vs ~1.0 s on the CPU, +~0.5 GB VRAM, cuDNN search HEURISTIC
       (0.9 s to load vs 4.1 s). CHOSEN. The int8 model stays the CPU fallback; a GPU failure puts the
       voice on the CPU for 10 minutes. pip check lists 5 "requires onnxruntime" lines: expected, the
       packages work with the GPU build (check_deps: wake word, whisper, chroma, piper all OK).
    Rollback: venv\Scripts\python -m pip uninstall -y onnxruntime-gpu, then pip install onnxruntime==1.31.0.

2f. Use the 12 GB (needs approval, each a small download)
  - small.en instead of base.en for speech recognition: fewer mishearings, about 0.5 GB.
  - A stronger chat model than llama3.2:3b: see Phase 6f. The card has room.
  RESULT (2026-10-14): small.en is now the default (code, Settings.inc, Control Center, Setup): 0.10 s a
    sentence on the RTX vs 0.04 s for base.en. Chat models, 4 short questions, all fully on the card:
      llama3.2:3b  0.06-0.42 s, 130-176 tok/s, 2.4 GiB, said 17x23 = 395 (wrong)
      phi4-mini    0.07-0.26 s (5.2 s first), 75-139 tok/s, 2.9 GiB, 391
      llama3.1:8b  0.10-0.71 s, 64-84 tok/s, 4.9 GiB, 391
    Recommended: llama3.1:8b (the Setup default). Still under 1 s; total VRAM ~6 GiB of 12 with voice + small.en.

Tests (fakes)
  - Whisper on a fake CPU plus gpu() turning True -> one reload on the card, none while busy, at most one
    a minute.
  - A fake cuda error in transcribe() -> that sentence is still answered, the card is tried again later.
  - JarvisGPU=require with gpu() False -> no model call, the fixed line.
  - The voice-on-CPU line is no longer a problem line.

Your live test after Phase 2
  1. Start JARVIS. The log shows "whisper base.en on GPU". No "CPU in use - speech recognition" line.
  2. Ask a short question. Time from the end of your sentence to his first word. Compare with Phase 0.
  3. Task Manager, GPU 1 (the RTX): activity while he listens and thinks.
  4. If you can, start him with the card off, then turn it on: within a minute the log shows the move.

Unverified until the live run: the speed gain, and the behaviour when the card is switched off.
Risk: more VRAM in use (about 1 GB for Whisper) while a game runs. The HUD tier already moves JARVIS to
the fast model on a busy GPU; JarvisWhisperDevice=cpu remains the manual override.
Rollback: set WHISPER DEVICE back to CPU. Revert the commit.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PHASE 3   HEARING YOU CORRECTLY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Fixes:  issue 10 (autocorrect, mishearing)
        issue 12 ("Hey Jarvis" doesn't work)
        issue 20 (part: which microphone he uses)
        issue 26 (other sound turned down while he listens)
Why third: Phases 4, 5 and 8 all depend on hearing short words like "stop" and "snooze".

3a. Whisper settings (no new package, about 8 lines in transcribe)
  - hotwords="Jarvis" and a short initial_prompt in command style, for example
    "Hey Jarvis, set a timer for ten minutes. Jarvis, turn the volume down."
    faster-whisper puts hotwords into the prompt of every window. initial_prompt reaches the first window
    only, which is enough for one short command. (Check in the installed version's docstring whether
    hotwords is ignored when a prefix is set. We set no prefix.)
  - beam_size 1 -> 5 (the library default), condition_on_previous_text=False, vad_filter=True (the Silero
    filter ships inside faster-whisper).
  - On the GPU (Phase 2) beam 5 costs almost nothing. On the CPU fall back to 3.

3b. Autocorrect layer (standard library only, about 30 lines)
  1. Name aliases (already in 1a).
  2. Command words. In a short sentence (8 words or fewer), a word that is nearly a command word is
     corrected with difflib.get_close_matches, cutoff 0.8, against the FIXED command vocabulary only
     (volume, mute, timer, alarm, snooze, reminder, bluetooth, microphone, brightness, connect ...).
     Sentences that go to chat are never rewritten.
  3. Low confidence. Whisper gives a score per segment (avg_logprob, no_speech_prob). A COMMAND heard
     with a low score is read back ("Volume to 30?") and needs a yes. Chat questions are not checked.
  4. Device and app names keep their own fuzzy match (best_app, and the Bluetooth match in Phase 7).
  5. Ignoring slight differences (the user asked for this instead of showing the heard text):
     - Filler is removed before matching: please, could you, can you, um, uh, so, like, just, for me,
       "I want you to", "go ahead and".
     - A table of sound-alikes for command sentences only: to/too/two, for/four, eight/ate, "won" = one,
       "the value" = volume, "time are" = timer, "a larm" = alarm.
     - Small words may be missing or extra: "set volume 30", "set the volume to 30", "volume at 30" and
       "put volume on 30" are one command.
     - If a sentence matches no command exactly but is within one corrected word of exactly ONE command,
       that command is taken. Within reach of two: he asks which. A risky action (shut down, close,
       forget everything) is never reached by a correction without its normal yes.
     - The tables live in one place in jarvis.py so a new mishearing is a one-line addition.
  shortcut: no learned corrections ("when you hear X, I mean Y"). Add a small file if the fixed list is
  not enough after a week of use.

3c. Wake word (diagnose with Phase 0, then about 20 lines)
  Possible causes, checked in this order:
    1. JarvisWakeWord is not 1, or the HUD tier is 3 or higher.
    2. The wake thread died in the start-up race (fixed by 1e).
    3. The model file is missing in the venv.
    4. The wrong microphone, or one that cannot do 16000 Hz.
    5. The threshold is too high for the room and the voice.
  Fixes, as far as the cause needs them:
    - The hologram says why it is off: "WAKE OFF: tier", "WAKE OFF: no model", "WAKE OFF: no microphone".
    - One log line a minute with the highest score seen (a number). This shows the right threshold.
    - A Control Center row for JarvisWakeSensitivity.
    - vad_threshold in openWakeWord (its built-in Silero speech check) against false triggers.
    - Optional, on the user's word: a custom verifier trained on the user's voice. Needs at least 3 short
      recordings of "Hey Jarvis" and about 10 seconds of other speech. They stay local; git ignores them.
    - Speex noise suppression is Linux only, so it is not an option.

3d. Which microphone (about 15 lines)
  - New setting JarvisMic: empty = the Windows default (today), or part of a device name.
  - Both input streams (l.632 and l.2243) use it. An unknown name falls back to the default and says so.
  - A Control Center row that cycles through the input devices sounddevice reports.
  - By voice, in Phase 7: "which microphone are you using", "use the headset microphone".

3e. Turn other sound down while he listens (issue 26; about 30 lines, no new package)
  - Music or a video in the room is a likely cause of mishearing. While the microphone is open for a
    TURN (not during idle wake-word listening), the Windows volume is lowered to JarvisDuckLevel percent
    of its current value, through the existing speakers() code, and put back before he speaks.
  - Order in a turn: chime, lower, listen, restore, think, speak.
  - Crash-safe: the old volume is written to a small file in Data\ before lowering and the file is
    removed after restoring. At start-up a leftover file means a crash: restore, then remove it.
  - A volume command in the same turn updates the stored value, so the restore does not undo it.
  - Not when muted, at volume 0, or when the volume is already below the level.
  - Settings JarvisDuck = 1 | 0 and JarvisDuckLevel = 30.
  - shortcut: it lowers the whole Windows volume, not single apps. Lowering per app (so a call stays
    loud) needs the per-app part of Core Audio, about 60 more lines. Upgrade if this annoys in use.
  - It does not help the wake word hear you over music. That is the sensitivity row in 3c.

Tests (fakes)
  - 3b.5: filler and sound-alike sentences route like the clean ones; a sentence between two commands
    asks; a chat sentence is never rewritten; no correction reaches shut down without a yes.
  - 3e: the fake volume goes down for the listen and is back before the fake voice; an exception in
    listen still restores; a leftover file at start restores once; "set volume to 30" in that turn ends
    at 30, not at the old value; nothing happens when muted.
  - The transcribe call carries hotwords, the prompt, beam 5 and the VAD filter.
  - "set the volyum to 30" is corrected; "three volumes of a book" in a chat sentence is not.
  - A low score on a command asks first; on a chat sentence it does not.
  - JarvisMic picks the matching fake device; an unknown name gives the default and one log line.
  - The wake loop reports the right "WAKE OFF" reason for each cause.

Your live test after Phase 3
  1. Say "Hey Jarvis" ten times from your normal seat. Count how often he wakes. Target: 9 of 10.
  2. Leave a video running for ten minutes. Count false wakes. Target: none.
  3. Say ten different commands with his name. Count how often the name is written correctly.
  4. Mumble a volume command. He should ask "Volume to ...?" instead of guessing.
  5. Say commands sloppily: "uh could you put volume on thirty please". It still works.
  6. Play music, wake him: the music drops while he listens and comes back when he answers.

Unverified until the live run: everything above, because it depends on the voice, room and microphone.
Rollback: each item is a setting or the arguments of one call.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PHASE 4   INTERRUPTING HIM
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Fixes:  issue 4  (keeps talking when I say stop or his name)
        issue 7  (doesn't let me interrupt in any case)
        issue 15 (can think Jarvis is speaking after I stop it)
        issue 23 (a stop key on the keyboard, only for interrupting)

4a. Stopping must be instant (no new package, about 25 lines)
  - play_samples(): write the sound in blocks of 50 ms and ABORT the stream on cancel, do not drain it.
  - _windows(): keep the helper process by handle and kill it on cancel (today it can run 60 seconds).
  - One function sets the HUD state. After a cancel it writes "idle" only when the stream is closed.
  - A cancelled turn never opens a follow-up.
  - This alone fixes issue 15 and makes the click stop feel immediate.

4a-2. A stop key on the keyboard (issue 23; about 25 lines, no new package)
  - ONLY for interruption. The key never wakes him and does nothing when he is idle.
  - The daemon registers one global hotkey with Windows (RegisterHotKey through ctypes) ONLY while he is
    speaking, thinking, listening or ringing, and releases it the moment he is idle. So the key keeps its
    normal meaning in every program the rest of the time.
  - A press does exactly what CANCEL does today: sound aborted (4a), alarm stopped, no follow-up.
  - Setting JarvisStopKey = End. CHOSEN by the user on 2026-10-13: the End key next to Home and PgUp on
    the Lenovo Legion 5i (Windows virtual key VK_END, 0x23). While registered, End does not reach the
    program in front (for example "jump to end of line" in an editor), but only while he is active.
  - Check first, read-only: that a press of that key reaches Windows as VK_END and not as an Fn-layer
    key. If it does not, ask the user before choosing another.
  - If Windows refuses the key (another program owns it): one log line and a HUD line, and everything
    else works as before.
  - Why not Rainmeter's own hotkey support: it would be active all the time and would wake him too.
  - During an alarm the key stops it. It does not snooze.

4b. Stopping by voice: three levels, the user picks

  The hard part: on speakers the microphone hears his own voice. Every guide found says the same: real
  barge-in needs echo cancellation with the played sound as the reference; without it, stay half-duplex
  or accept false stops. Loudness-only detection triggers on his own voice.

  Level                What it does                                        Speakers   New package
  -------------------  --------------------------------------------------  ---------  -----------------
  wake (default)       The wake-word model keeps running while he speaks.  yes        no
                       "Hey Jarvis" stops him and he listens. Threshold
                       raised during speech. A sentence of his own that
                       contains his name is ignored.
  voice                Any speech from you for 0.5 s stops him (Silero     no, head-  no
                       speech check on the microphone).                    phones only
  aec                  Echo cancellation first, then "voice" works on      yes        yes: a WebRTC audio
                       speakers too.                                                  package. Experiment,
                                                                                      size and Windows
                                                                                      support shown first.

  - New setting JarvisBargeIn = off | wake | voice. Default wake.
  - ANSWERED by the user on 2026-10-13: they use both speakers and headphones. So the default stays
    "wake" (safe on speakers) and the Control Center gets a BARGE-IN row to switch to "voice" when the
    headphones are on. Optional later: switch by itself when the default output device is a headset
    (needs the device kind from Windows; unverified). "aec" stays an approval-gated experiment.
  - One microphone owner stays true: the wake loop owns the microphone during speech; playback never
    opens it. Change needed: wake_loop() must keep the stream while `busy` is held ONLY in the speaking
    state, and still drop it while a turn is recording.
  - After a voice stop: "stop / quiet / never mind / enough" just ends it. Anything else is a new command.
  - Stated plainly: at level "wake" the bare word "stop" is NOT heard while he speaks. "Hey Jarvis" or a
    click is. Bare "stop" needs "voice" (headphones) or "aec".

Tests (fakes)
  - Cancel in mid-sentence: within 200 ms no more sound is written, the state is idle, `busy` is free,
    no follow-up listen starts.
  - The fake Windows-voice process is killed on cancel.
  - The stop key: registered when a fake turn starts, released when it ends; a fake press cancels a
    fake speech and a fake alarm; a press while idle does nothing and never starts a turn; a refused
    registration leaves a turn working.
  - A fake wake score during fake playback aborts it and opens exactly one listen.
  - His own sentence containing "Jarvis" does not stop him.
  - Microphone sessions never overlap. Level "off" behaves exactly as today.

Your live test after Phase 4
  1. Ask for something long ("tell me about black holes"). Click the hologram. He stops within a blink
     and the hologram goes idle at once.
  2. Same question. Say "Hey Jarvis" over him. He stops and listens.
  3. Let him say a sentence with his own name in it ("who are you?"). He must not stop himself.
  4. With headphones and level "voice": just start talking over him. He stops.
  5. Press the stop key while he speaks: he stops at once. Press it while he is idle: nothing happens,
     and the key does its normal job in the program in front. If nothing happens while he speaks, the
     End key may be on an Fn layer on your keyboard (or another app holds it: Control Center shows "Stop
     key: END is taken"). Tell Claude before another key is picked.
  6. Press it while an alarm rings: the alarm ends.
  7. On speakers, level "wake": let him talk for a while. His own voice must not stop him. If it does,
     set BARGE-IN to OFF and report it.

Unverified until the live run: false stops from his own voice on your speakers. Fakes cannot prove this.
Rollback: JarvisBargeIn=off. Revert the commit.

RESULT (built 2026-10-14, committed in 301c22f, NOT live)
  4a. Instant stop
    - One stop function (stop_now) for the click, the End key and talking over him: it sets the stop flag,
      puts the HUD on idle at once and writes "stop: click / stop key / barge-in" to the log.
    - The sound is aborted, not drained. Measured on this laptop: silent 82 ms after the stop (Kokoro, 1 s
      into a 5.4 s sentence). Target was under 200 ms.
    - The Windows voice helper (PowerShell) is killed at the stop. Before, it could run up to 60 s.
    - Sentences already made are no longer spoken after a stop.
    - A late "speaking" / "thinking" / "listening" from a turn that is ending can no longer show after a stop.
    - The HUD goes idle as soon as he finishes talking. Before, it waited for the GPU check after the reply
      (the likely cause of issue 15).
    - No follow-up listen after a stop (issue 23's "a stop opens a follow-up anyway").
    - "Quiet", "never mind" and "enough" now end a follow-up, like "stop" and "thanks".
  4a-2. Stop key: End, held only while he is busy (talking, thinking, listening or ringing). Windows
    accepted End as a hotkey on this laptop (registered and released once, no key press). A refused key
    gives one log line and a Control Center line, and everything else keeps working.
  4b. Barge-in, in the wake-word loop (one microphone owner, as before):
    - wake (default): "Hey Jarvis" over him stops him and he listens once. While he talks the wake
      threshold is raised by 0.2 (at most 0.95), and for the length of his own sentence that has "Jarvis"
      in it (+1 s) the wake word is ignored.
    - voice: about 0.56 s of speech (7 blocks of 80 ms, speech score 0.5 or more) stops him. He then
      listens as a follow-up, so "stop / quiet / never mind / enough" just ends it. With no speech filter
      loaded it acts like "wake" and logs that once.
    - off: the wake word drops the microphone while he is busy, exactly as before.
  Control Center: BARGE-IN (OFF / WAKE / VOICE) and STOP KEY (END / OFF) rows.
  Checks: test_jarvis OK (py -3 and venv), check_voice OK, route check 0 differences of 65, Lua 5.1 parse
  OK. Mutation check: undoing each of the 11 fixes, one at a time, made the tests fail.
  Rollback: JarvisBargeIn=off, JarvisStopKey=off; before a commit the before_phase4/ copies (scratchpad).

Found while building Phases 2-4 (new issues)
  N1. FIXED. A click-stop while the AI fallback ("One moment.") was still reading the request let the
      command run anyway. Now nothing runs after a stop.
  N2. FIXED. The HUD showed "speaking" while the GPU check ran after the reply (cause of issue 15).
  N3. FIXED. Sentences already made were still spoken after a stop, one by one.
  N4. FIXED. The Windows voice kept going up to 60 s after a stop.
  N5. FIXED (Phase 2). whisper() had no lock, so the preload and a turn could load the model twice.
  N6. FIXED (Phase 3). The "Jarvis" hotword made a short "Yes." come out as "Jarvis Yes."; hear() now
      strips the name.
  N7. WON'T FIX (user's call 2026-10-14: cosmetic, not cared about). Flaky test, not a code bug.
      test_jarvis l.1248 "add 5 minutes to the timer" fails about 1 run in 4 under the venv.
      Cause (re-checked 2026-10-14): save_reminders stores the due time rounded to the second (:.0f),
      the "add" path computes the new due from that stored value and replies span(due - now) with the
      real clock. Rounding (up to 0.5 s) plus the time between the two commands can give 899 s, so he
      says "14 minutes 59 seconds" instead of "15 minutes". The timer still rings at the right second.
      span() already rounds, so the old idea "round the time left" would not help. If it is ever
      fixed: freeze time.time in those 3 test lines (test only, about 3 lines). A failure of only this
      assert in a test run is N7, not a regression.
  N8. NOT A BUG for Rainmeter. control.lua does not parse under Lua 5.5 (a for-loop variable is assigned,
      l.~682). Rainmeter uses Lua 5.1, where it is fine. Already like this before Phase 3.
  N9. LIMIT. At level "wake" a bare "stop" is not heard while he talks; "Hey Jarvis", a click or End is.
      Bare "stop" needs level "voice" (headphones).
  N10. LIMIT. If the AI fallback is reading when you stop him, its call finishes in the background (at most
      8 s) and the answer is thrown away. Nothing runs, but the GPU is busy that long.
  N11. LIMIT. After talking over him, the new turn waits for the stopped one to finish (at most 5 s, it is
      usually the GPU check). If it takes longer, he logs "barge-in: the stopped turn didn't end" and does
      not listen. Say "Hey Jarvis" again.
  N12. LIMIT. The STOP KEY row offers END or OFF only. A hand-typed other value in Settings.inc shows END in
      the row but registers nothing.
  N13. TEST BUG (found 2026-10-14 while building 6a; FIXED later on 2026-10-14, see AUDIT 2 in the REDESIGN). [Also in BUGS TO WATCH OUT FOR, top.] test_jarvis l.1238 "set an alarm for 7 am"
      expects "You already have an alarm for 7:00 AM." and the "move my alarm" line after it expects no
      "tomorrow". After 7 AM local time the reply adds "tomorrow", so the run stops there and every test
      after it (Phase 4 barge-in, 6a training) is skipped. Fails at commit 8494cee too, so not a regression.
      Workaround used: run with TZ=ABC+12 (local time before 7 AM). Fix if wanted: freeze the clock or
      accept "tomorrow" in those lines (test only, about 3 lines).
      UPDATE 2026-10-14 (audit fixes): at 19:01 UTC, TZ=ABC+12 gives 07:01 local and the run failed EARLIER,
      at the "Alarms set for 7:00 AM ... and 8:00 AM" assert (HEAD l.1247); TZ=ABC+3/+5/+9/-4 failed at the
      "already have an alarm for 7:00 AM" assert (HEAD l.1729). Same on HEAD, so not a regression.
      TZ=ABC-8 (03:01 local) passed. So no fixed TZ always works; the real fix (freeze the clock) is worth it.
  N14. CHECK SCRIPT. [Also in BUGS TO WATCH OUT FOR, top.] check_route.py flags "remind me to cook in 20 minutes" as different although both
      sides print the same tuple (a time-based due value). route() was not touched in 6a. Treat this one
      line as noise until the script compares without the timestamp.
  N15. LIMIT (control.lua, the row drawer `for b = 1, 4`). [Also in BUGS TO WATCH OUT FOR, top.] A Control Center row shows at most 4 buttons; a
      5th is silently not drawn. That is why TRAIN got its own WAKE TRAINING row. New buttons: new row.
  N16. LIMIT (6a). A click or End stops the training only between clips, so up to 15 s late (the 15 s
      "talk normally" clip). The "train the wake word" voice phrase is not built; the button only.
  N17. LIMIT (Phase 6a). Blocks are picked by keywords (since 2026-10-14 also by meaning, cut-off 0.6): false hits ("speed test" adds the calendar, "hotel"
      the PC readings) only add data; misses ("anything Friday" before the widening) leave data out and he
      says he doesn't have it. A follow-up with no keyword relies on the question before it.
  N18. LIMIT (Phase 6a). The Pending line goes in only for time / setting words (or a PC question). A
      question about alarms with none of them reaches the model without the schedule; the Phase 1 CLAIM
      guard is then the only check.
  N19. OPEN (Phase 5b). "again in ten" is listed as a snooze phrase in 5b but is not recognised as one.
      Not fixed; the other snooze phrases work in test_jarvis.


========================================================================================================
BEFORE PHASE 5   THE USER'S LIVE TEST OF EVERYTHING BUILT SO FAR (added 2026-10-14)
========================================================================================================
Nothing of Rounds 1, 2, 4 or Phases 2, 3, 4 has been heard on the real microphone and speakers yet. Phase 5
changes the alarm loop, the stop path and the wake word's role while ringing, so these tests come first: a
problem found now is cheaper to fix than one found under Phase 5.

S. SET-UP (once)
  S1. Tell Claude "copy to live". Claude lists every file it will copy into the live skin first, keeps a
      backup of the live files, and copies only on your word. (Claude never copies without it.)
      Or do it yourself with the "COPY TO LIVE" commands just below.

  COPY TO LIVE (PowerShell, run yourself; one block at a time, all in the SAME window)
    Copies the COMMITTED version (git HEAD), not the working folder, so half-done edits never go live.
    Repo:      C:\Users\Senai\RainmeterSpaceship
    Live skin: C:\Users\Senai\Rainmeter\Skins\Spaceship
    Skipped on purpose: @Resources\Data\ (memory, reminders, logs; only Data\zones.lua is in git and it is
    already live), @Resources\Backups\, Settings.inc (the committed copy has the personal paths BLANK, so
    it is never copied over; step 3b instead adds any setting live is missing, from the repo's working
    copy, and never changes a value live already has), State.inc and Apps.ini (the skin rewrites both at
    runtime). /E without /MIR only adds and overwrites, it never deletes
    live files. git archive stamps every file with the commit time, so /XO still lets the copy overwrite
    older live files. Icons are not in git, so the runtime icons are never touched.
    Not in git: the Vosk stop-word model (Jarvis\vosk, about 40 MB) - step 4 copies it.
    The venv (%LOCALAPPDATA%\SpaceshipHUD\venv) is shared by repo and live; it already has vosk and
    pywebrtc-audio, so nothing is installed.

    0. Check what HEAD is (it is what goes live; uncommitted changes do NOT go live):
         git -C C:\Users\Senai\RainmeterSpaceship log --oneline -1
         git -C C:\Users\Senai\RainmeterSpaceship status --short
    1. Back up the live skin (outside Skins\, so Rainmeter does not load it as a skin):
         $repo = "C:\Users\Senai\RainmeterSpaceship"
         $src  = "$repo\Skins\Spaceship"
         $live = "C:\Users\Senai\Rainmeter\Skins\Spaceship"
         $stamp = Get-Date -Format yyyyMMdd-HHmmss
         $bak  = "C:\Users\Senai\Rainmeter\Spaceship-backup-$stamp"
         robocopy $live $bak /E /R:1 /W:1 /NFL /NDL
         "robocopy exit $LASTEXITCODE  (0-7 = OK, 8+ = failed)"
    2. Export HEAD to a temp folder (only if step 1 said OK):
         $tmp = "$env:TEMP\spaceship-export-$stamp"
         git -C $repo archive --format=tar -o "$tmp.tar" HEAD Skins/Spaceship
         "git exit $LASTEXITCODE  (0 = OK)"
         New-Item -ItemType Directory $tmp | Out-Null
         & "$env:WINDIR\System32\tar.exe" -xf "$tmp.tar" -C $tmp
         "tar exit $LASTEXITCODE  (0 = OK)"
    3. Copy the export over live (only if step 2 said OK twice):
         robocopy "$tmp\Skins\Spaceship" $live /E /XO /R:1 /W:1 /XD "$tmp\Skins\Spaceship\@Resources\Data" "$tmp\Skins\Spaceship\@Resources\Backups" /XF Settings.inc State.inc Apps.ini
         "robocopy exit $LASTEXITCODE  (0-7 = OK, 8+ = failed)"
    3b. Add new settings to live Settings.inc (keeps every live value; your Control Center choices stay).
        Reads the repo's WORKING copy (it has your personal values; the committed one has them blank):
         $rs = "$src\@Resources\Settings.inc"; $ls = "$live\@Resources\Settings.inc"
         $have = @{}; foreach ($l in Get-Content $ls) { if ($l -match '^\s*([^;\[\s][^=]*)=') { $have[$matches[1].Trim()] = 1 } }
         $add = @(); $note = @()
         foreach ($l in Get-Content $rs) {
           if ($l -match '^\s*;') { $note += $l }
           elseif ($l -match '^\s*([^\[\s][^=]*)=') { if (-not $have[$matches[1].Trim()]) { $add += $note + $l }; $note = @() }
           else { $note = @() } }
         if ($add) { Add-Content -LiteralPath $ls -Value $add -Encoding ASCII }
         "added $(@($add | Where-Object { $_ -notmatch '^\s*;' }).Count) setting(s)"
       Live Settings.inc is plain ASCII with one [Variables] section, so adding at the end is safe.
    4. Copy the Vosk model (not in git; skip if live already has @Resources\Jarvis\vosk):
         robocopy "$src\@Resources\Jarvis\vosk" "$live\@Resources\Jarvis\vosk" /E /R:1 /W:1 /NFL /NDL
         "robocopy exit $LASTEXITCODE  (0-7 = OK, 8+ = failed)"
       If the repo has no vosk folder either: run Setup-Jarvis.ps1 in the live Jarvis folder; step 4b2 asks
       before it downloads.
    5. Remove the temp export:  Remove-Item -Recurse -Force $tmp, "$tmp.tar"
    6. Reload: & "C:\Program Files\Rainmeter\Rainmeter.exe" !RefreshApp
       then restart JARVIS and wait for STANDBY. ShipCore.exe rebuilds itself on load when ShipCore.cs is
       newer (core.ps1).
    UNDO (same window, or set $bak/$live again to the backup folder's name first):
         robocopy $bak $live /E /R:1 /W:1
       then refresh Rainmeter and restart JARVIS. Undo puts old files back but does not delete files the
       copy added (e.g. Jarvis\vosk); they are harmless. It also restores Settings.inc as it was before
       step 3b.
  S2. Put kokoro-v1.0.onnx next to kokoro-v1.0.int8.onnx in the live voices folder, or re-run Setup.
  S3. Control Center > JARVIS: WHISPER DEVICE = AUTO, WHISPER = small.en, MODEL = llama3.1:8b.
      Leave BARGE-IN = WAKE, STOP KEY = END, DUCK MUSIC = ON, MICROPHONE = your usual one.
  S4. Restart JARVIS (or Rainmeter). Wait until the hologram says STANDBY.
  S5. Speakers for every test, unless the test says headphones.

How to report: for each test write its number, PASS or FAIL, and for a FAIL what you said, what he said
or did, and roughly when. Claude does not read the log or Data\ (privacy rule), so if a test asks for log
lines, copy just those lines from Data\jarvis.log yourself. They hold numbers and states, not your words,
except "you: ..." lines: leave those out if you prefer.

A. START-UP AND THE GPU (Phase 2, 2e, 2f)
  A1. Start JARVIS. Log: "whisper small.en on GPU"; no "CPU in use - speech recognition" line.
      Report: the whisper line and any "CPU" line.
  A2. Ask "what's the capital of France?". Time from the end of your sentence to his first word (a phone
      stopwatch is fine). Do it 3 times. Report the 3 times and the "timing: reply start -> first audio"
      log lines. Phase 0 baseline was 15-28 s for chat replies.
  A3. Task Manager > Performance > GPU 1 (the RTX): activity while he listens, thinks and speaks.
      Report: yes / no for each, and the dedicated memory in use (about 6 GB expected).
  A4. Ask something that needs a longer reply. The voice sounds like before (Kokoro, the voice you picked),
      no Windows voice. Report any Windows voice or crackle.
  A5. Optional: start him with the laptop in a mode where the RTX is off, then switch it on. Within about a
      minute the log shows Whisper moving to the GPU. Report the line, or "not tried".
  A6. Hologram: only J.A.R.V.I.S plus STANDBY / LISTENING / THINKING / SPEAKING. No reply text, no error
      text on it. Report anything else you see on it.

B. HEARING YOU (Phase 3)
  B1. "Hey Jarvis" 10 times from your normal seat, normal voice, about 10 s apart. Report: how many woke
      him (target 9 of 10). Also the "wake: best 0.xx this minute" log lines.
  B2. Leave a video or music playing for 10 minutes without talking. Report: false wakes (target 0).
  B3. 10 commands with his name ("Jarvis, set the volume to 30", "Jarvis, pause the music", ...).
      Report: how many he did right.
  B4. Mumble a volume command on purpose. He should ask "Did you say ...?" instead of acting. Answer "yes"
      once and "no" once. Report what he did.
  B5. Sloppy: "uh could you put volume on thirty please". Report: did it work.
  B6. Play music, wake him: the music goes down while he listens and comes back before he answers.
      Report: did it duck, did it come back to the same level.
  B7. Play music, wake him, say "set the volume to 50". Afterwards the volume is 50, not the old level.
  B8. Control Center > MICROPHONE: step through it once. Each name is one of your devices; set it back to
      your usual one. Report any name that is wrong or missing.
  B9. Say just "Yes." after "Hey Jarvis". He must not hear "Jarvis Yes.". Report what he answered.

C. STOPPING HIM (Phase 4)
  C1. "Tell me about black holes." Click the hologram mid-sentence. He stops within a blink; the hologram
      shows STANDBY at once; no follow-up listen starts (no chime).
  C2. Same question. Say "Hey Jarvis" over him. He stops, chimes and listens. Ask something short.
  C3. "Who are you?" (his answer has "Jarvis" in it). He must not stop himself.
  C4. Speakers, BARGE-IN = WAKE: let him give a long answer to the end, 3 times. Report any time he stopped
      by himself (a false barge-in). If it happens: set BARGE-IN = OFF and report the "barge-in" log lines.
  C5. Headphones, BARGE-IN = VOICE: start talking over him (no "Hey Jarvis"). He stops and listens. Say
      "never mind": he ends silently. Then again, and ask a new question instead: he answers it.
      Set BARGE-IN back to WAKE afterwards.
  C6. Press End while he speaks: he stops at once. Press End while he is idle: nothing happens, and in an
      editor End still jumps to the end of the line. If End does nothing while he speaks: report it (it may
      be on the Fn layer, or Control Center shows "Stop key: END is taken"). Do not pick another key yet.
  C7. Press End while he is thinking (right after you ask a long question): he stops, says nothing.
  C8. "Set a timer for 1 minute." When it rings, press End: it ends, no snooze.
  C9. Ask a question so he answers and listens for a follow-up; say "quiet" / "enough": he ends silently.
  C10. Click while he says "One moment." (a sloppy command that needs the AI fallback, e.g. "could you make
      it a bit louder for me"): nothing runs afterwards (the volume does not change).

D. ROUNDS 1, 2 AND 4 (commands; built earlier, never live-tested)
  D1. "Set an alarm for 7 and 8 am", then "turn off my alarm": he asks "Which one?". Say "the 7".
  D2. "Undo that": the 7:00 alarm is back. "What alarms do I have?" lists both.
  D3. "Remind me to call mom tomorrow at 9": set for TOMORROW, label "to call mom".
  D4. "Set an alarm for half past six", then "no, PM": it moves to 6:30 PM.
  D5. "Set a timer for 10 minutes", "add 5 minutes to the timer" (he says 15, or "14 minutes 59 seconds": see N7),
      "make the timer 2 minutes", "undo that".
  D6. "An alarm for 7 and a timer for 10 minutes" in one sentence: both are set.
  D7. "Set an alarm for 7" twice: the second time "You already have an alarm for 7:00 AM."
  D8. After a reply, in the follow-up: "thanks" ends it silently. On a turn you started, "thanks" gets
      a reply.
  D9. "Turn it up by 20", then "lower the volume by 5 percent".
  D10. "Snooze" when nothing rang: "Nothing is ringing right now."
  D11. "What reminders do I have?" lists alarms and reminders together, nothing invented.
  D12. Let a 1-minute alarm ring; say "stop" (no name) in it: it stops (today's ring; Phase 5 replaces it).

E. OLD FEATURES STILL WORK (regressions)
  E1. "What time is it?", "what's 15 percent of 240?", "how many kilometres is 26 miles?": quick and right.
  E2. Media: "pause the music", "next song", "play".
  E3. To-do: "add milk to my to-do list", "what's on my to-do list?".
  E4. "Remember that my favourite colour is blue", then later "what's my favourite colour?".
  E5. The daily briefing (if you use it) and the HUD readings (CPU / GPU / network) still update.
  E6. Control Center: every JARVIS row still clicks and saves (PERSONALITY, VOICE, FOLLOW-UP, WAKE ...).
  E7. Leave it idle 15 minutes: the speech models unload (JarvisIdleUnload), the next "Hey Jarvis"
      still works.
  E8. Mute Windows and let a 1-minute alarm come due: a pop-up only, as you chose.

  F2. The same while he answers a long question.

  What the live test found (user's words, in short)
    L1. During an alarm he can't be interrupted; he only listens after it. He should listen while it rings,
        ignoring his own voice.
    L2. "Hey Jarvis" works about 5 of 10 times, and only when said loud and clear. Lower the threshold.
    L3. He takes things as commands that aren't. Unclear: he should ask. Very unclear: don't take it.
    L4. He lied about changing the volume (it worked before, now it doesn't).

  Cause of L4, confirmed from the user's log (2026-10-14 09:44:30)
    - Heard right: "Okay Jarvis, um, just be quiet, turn up." No "interpreter:" line follows, so the line never
      reached interpret(): ask_ai() needs a word from AI_WORDS (jarvis.py 590), which has "quieter" but not
      "quiet", and nothing for a bare "turn up". So it went to chat.
    - Chat said "Making it a bit more lively ... Speaker volume upped to 90%." CLAIM (1675) has no "upped",
      so the guard missed it (checked by running the regex on both sentences). Nothing ran; the volume did
      not change. The next line, "set the volume to 20%", matched route() directly and worked.
    - Other holes found in the code (not seen in the log yet):
      * interpret() fails (8 s timeout, or a check: "not what was said", "no preset", "volume not said") ->
        the line goes to chat, and chat can claim. CLAIM also misses "I've lowered the volume", "Volume
        lowered to 20 percent", "Turning the volume down now", "Sure, I'll turn it down", "Done, the
        volume's lowered" (checked).
      * interpret()'s volume_step (667) is "down" only for down/lower/quieter/decrease/reduce/softer, else UP:
        "the music is too loud" -> turn it up. A said step ("down 20") is dropped (always 10).
      * The "Did you say ...?" gate (3043) is only for kind "act": alarms, timers, reminders and multi run on
        a guess when Whisper was unsure.
    - L5 answer: there is already ONE execution path (route -> command_reply / run_action). The reinterpreted
      ones fail BEFORE it: they fall into chat, or the AI's sentence loses the direction or amount.

  User's decisions (2026-10-14)
    D1. He asks "Did you say ...?" only when Whisper was unsure (heard_score < -1.0), for EVERY command kind,
        AI reading or not. A confident AI reading runs without asking.
    D2. Follow-up: no cap. He keeps listening after each answer until you stop answering (silence), say an
        ender ("thanks", "that's all"), or stop him.
    D3. Chat that sounds like a command is sent to the interpreter instead of lying, AND the guards stay.

  R5-1  One command path; never a claim from chat (L3, L4, L5; D1, D3). In turn() and its helpers.

    b. interpret() tells "the model said chat" (None, as today) from "it looked like a command but failed"

    c. The command block in turn() (3043-3075: the gate, multi, command_reply, the two-phase confirm) becomes
       one nested function run_command(...). It is the only place a command runs: route() hits, autocorrect,
       interpret() and the chat redirect (d) all call it. (L5: one pipeline.)
    d. Chat redirect (D3): when no_claims() catches a claim and interpret() was NOT already tried on this

       (a) makes this rare, because such lines go to interpret() first.

       a change word in any tense (up, down, upped, raised, lowered, increased, decreased, reduced, set,
       turned, turning, setting, changed, muted, paused, skipped, a percent), plus "I'll turn/set/...".
       Risk: a harmless "you could turn the volume down" is replaced too. A false "nothing changed" beats
       a false "done".
    f. The gate (D1): Whisper unsure -> "Did you say: <what was heard>?" for act, remind (set / cancel / flip)
       and multi; "yes" runs it through run_command, anything else -> "Cancelled." Lists and the volume
       level (read only) are not asked.

  R5-2  Volume direction and amount from the AI (L4). In interpret() line 667.

    - The cap "follow < 3" (3149) goes. The recursion turn() -> turn() becomes a loop, so endless follow-ups

    - One log line per end: "follow-up: ended (silence | empty | ender | muted | no-follow reply | cancelled |
      busy)". "busy" = a reminder took the lock in the gap.

    0.45 this minute". So R5-5 goes ahead. Also seen: "whisper base.en on GPU", not small.en as planned for the live
    test: check Control Center > WHISPER.

  R5-6  Wake word: the normal filter plus three trained checks (user's design 2026-10-14; it replaces the
    earlier ideas). Built BEFORE R5-1 (user's order). In wake_loop() plus a training step.
    CHANGED 2026-10-14: step 6b below (W1-W3) is REPLACED by FIX ROUND 5 N1 (before Phase 7): W2 and W3
    are removed, and "Hey" then "Jarvis" within 5 s takes their place. Step 6a (training) stays, with
    one change: the "hey_pause" style becomes "hey" (your bare "Hey"); see N1.
    How openWakeWord 0.6.0 really works (read in the installed library, model.py / custom_verifier_model.py):
      - hey_jarvis gives ONE score per 80 ms for the whole phrase. There is no separate "Hey" or "Jarvis" model.
      - A "custom verifier" is a tiny logistic-regression model (scikit-learn, already in the venv) on the
        audio features openWakeWord already computes. Its built-in use REPLACES the score, so we call our
        verifiers ourselves next to the normal score: model.preprocessor.get_features(...) ->
        verifier.predict_proba(...). One small sum per candidate; no Whisper, no GPU.
      - The library's own training keeps only clip moments scoring >= 0.5, so a soft or bare "Jarvis" would
        give no examples. Our training uses its helpers with a low floor instead.
      - With vad_threshold set, the library zeroes every score unless its speech filter saw speech 0.4-0.56 s
        earlier. So all paths still need real speech.
    The four ways to wake (any one is enough):
      W0. Normal (as today): score >= JarvisWakeSensitivity (0.35).
      W1. "Hey Jarvis" said softly: score >= 0.1 AND your "Hey Jarvis" check >= 0.5. No silence rule.
      W2. "Hey ... Jarvis" (a pause between): score >= 0.1 AND your "Hey ... Jarvis" check >= 0.5. No silence rule.
      W3. A bare "Jarvis": score >= 0.1 AND your "Jarvis" check >= 0.5 AND no speech for 2 s BEFORE AND no
          speech for 1 s AFTER (the speech filter, so music or a fan does not count). So a bare "Jarvis"
          wakes him about 1 s later than "Hey Jarvis", and "Jarvis" inside a sentence never does.
      The floor 0.1 and the 0.5 checks become settings (JarvisWakeEasy, 0 = W1-W3 off; JarvisWakeCheck).
      Log: "wake word heard (0.xx, W0|W1|W2|W3)", so we see which way woke him.
    Training (6a): WAKE WORD > TRAIN in Control Center (or "train the wake word"). He says what to do and you
      say: "Hey Jarvis" 6 times, "Hey ... Jarvis" 6 times, a bare "Jarvis" 6 times (each 3 s window, with a
      ding), then talk normally for 15 s (the "not Jarvis" examples). Three check files are saved in Data\
      (wake_hey_jarvis.pkl, wake_hey_pause.pkl, wake_jarvis.pkl). As built: the recordings stay in
      memory and are never written to disk (nothing to delete). Voice phrase not built (N16). The log shows the
      highest normal score per style, e.g. "train: bare Jarvis peaks 0.04": if a style never reaches the 0.1
      floor, its check is not made and he says so (that way stays off; the others still work).
    Order: 6a training + the measurement first (no wake change yet). You train once and send the "train:"
      lines. Then 6b (W1-W3 in wake_loop), with the floor set from those numbers. Then R5-5 only if needed.
    Risks / limits
      - Tuned to YOUR voice and room. Anyone else, or a new mic, gets W0 only. Retrain after a mic change. The
        docs: a verifier is "not intended to perform the task of speaker verification" - not a lock.
      - A .pkl file can run code when loaded. Only the three files the training made, in the private Data\
        folder, are ever loaded; nothing from elsewhere.
      - W3 can still fire on a video that says just "Jarvis" with a pause each side (rare).
      - Unverified until the training runs: whether a bare "Jarvis" scores above 0.1 at all, and how much
        W1-W3 raise 7 of 10.
    Tests (fakes): each of W0-W3 wakes; W3 with speech 1 s before or 0.5 s after does not; a check at 0.3 does
      not; no .pkl -> only W0; JarvisWakeEasy=0 -> only W0; training deletes its clips; a style that never
      reaches the floor makes no file.
    Live test: train; B1 for each style (10x at a normal voice, aim 9+ each); B2 (10 min of video, 0 false
      wakes); someone else / a video saying "Hey Jarvis" softly should NOT wake him through W1-W3.
    Rollback: git revert; delete the three Data\wake_*.pkl; JarvisWakeEasy=0.

RESULT (as built; the spec below is what was asked for)
  - alarm() rings an alarm (any alarm in the batch): a ding about every 2 s with 1.5 s listening gaps
    (hear(patience=1.5)); the line again every ALARM_SAY = 30 s; ALARM_CAP = 300 s, then "alarm missed" in the
    log and the pop-up line "Missed alarm from <time>."; the name alone (NAME_ONLY) stops it in a gap.
  - Alarm snooze says "Snoozed until <time>."; SNOOZE = 300. Timers and reminders keep the RINGS loop.
  - 5c needed no code; a test checks the answers come from the reminders file.

Fixes:  issue 9  (the ring lasts no time; it should be continuous and keep going)
        issue 16 (snooze doesn't work)
        issue 8  (asking about alarms that are set)

5a. Continuous ring (about 35 lines, a second loop in alarm(), for ALARMS only - Q11)
  - If any due item is an alarm, the batch rings continuously: today's ding(), then 1.5 s of gap
    listening, repeated (a ding about every 2 s). Timers and reminders keep the RINGS loop unchanged.
  - He says the label at the start and again every 30 s (ALARM_SAY), not after every ding.
  - It stops on: a click, End, barge-in while the ding/label plays, a stop or snooze word in a gap, or the
    name alone ("Jarvis", "Hey Jarvis") heard in a gap.
  - The gaps are how "stop" works WITHOUT the wake word. _listen's loudness check runs in each gap; only
    if someone speaks are the words transcribed (hear(patience=1.5)).
  - Safety cap: 5 minutes (ALARM_CAP, Q6), then "alarm missed" is logged and a pop-up line
    "Missed alarm from <time>." is shown (Q10); "snooze" in the next 5 minutes still works.
  - The next time of a repeating alarm is still saved BEFORE the ring starts, so a crash cannot make one
    ring for ever.
  - No follow-up listening while an alarm rings, as Alexa and Google do.
  - Muted or STEALTH: no sound and no listening, the pop-up line only, as today.

5b. Snooze (about 10 lines)
  - It works now because the alarm is real (1b) and listening in the gaps is cheap (5a).
  - Phrases: "snooze", "five more minutes", "ten more minutes", "again in ten", "later", "not now",
    "snooze half an hour". Default 5 minutes (SNOOZE = 300, Q8). Allowed range 1 minute to 2 hours.
  - Snooze is checked before the stop words, so "okay, snooze" snoozes.
  - An alarm confirms with the real new time: "Snoozed until 7:11 AM." Timers keep "Snoozed for ...".

5c. Asking about alarms (no code: reminder_cmd already does it; checked by a test)
  - "Do I have an alarm set", "what alarms are set", "when is my next alarm", "what timers are running"
    are answered by code from the reminders file, never by the model.

Tests (fake clock, fake sound, fake hear)
  - The ring continues past 60 fake seconds.
  - "stop" in a gap ends it. "snooze 5 minutes" saves it again and it rings again at the new time.
  - Room speech that is not a stop word: the ring resumes.
  - Silence reaches the 5-minute cap: "alarm missed" logged, the pop-up line shown; the label is said
    every 30 s.
  - A click ends it. "Hey Jarvis" in a gap ends it; "what time is it" does not.
  - A timer still rings RINGS rounds and says "Snoozed for". A mixed batch with an alarm rings long.
  - The list answer matches the file. A repeating alarm is back for tomorrow after firing.
  - The old tests for repeating reminders still pass.

Your live test after Phase 5
  1. "Set an alarm for one minute." It rings and keeps ringing. Wait 30 seconds: still ringing.
  2. Say "stop" (no name). It ends.
  3. Again; say "snooze". He says "Snoozed until ...". It rings again five minutes later.
  4. Again; say "five more minutes". Then ask "do I have an alarm set?" He names the new time.
  5. Again; click the hologram. It ends. Again; say "Hey Jarvis" in a gap. It ends.
  6. Again; leave it. It stops after 5 minutes and the pop-up shows "Missed alarm from ...".
  7. A 1-minute timer behaves exactly as before.

Risk: room speech in a gap pauses the ring for a second or two. It resumes unless the words are a stop or
snooze word.
Rollback: revert the commit. The reminders file format is unchanged.

6a. Only send what the question needs (about 40 lines)   BUILT 2026-10-14 (S1), not live
  context() is split into blocks. A block is added only when the question touches it:

    Block           Added when the question has
    --------------  ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Time and date   always (one line)
    Calendar        schedule, today, tomorrow, tonight, week, class, quiz, exam, meeting, free, busy,
                    "when is", "what's on"; added in the build: day names, weekend, month, morning,
                    afternoon, evening, later, due, deadline, "anything on / for / happening"
    Mail count      mail, email, inbox, unread, message
    To-dos          to-do, todo, task, "my list"
    PC readings     cpu, gpu, temperature, hot, ram, memory, fan, power, battery (and the sensor lines:
                    HWiNFO values, CPU and GPU load, volume, brightness, mic, disk, Pending)
    Pending line    alarm, timer, remind, wake, snooze, cancel, set, "did you", o'clock, "7 am", minutes,
                    hours, morning, tonight, later. Without a PC word it goes in alone, labelled "don't
                    bring it up unless asked about alarms, reminders or timers", so a claim can be checked
    Network         wifi, network, internet, connection
    Open apps       open, running, window, app
    Profile         a question about the user (my, me, I; not "me" after tell/give/show/help/let) -
                    capped at 500 characters
    Briefing        all blocks and the whole profile, as today

  Plus one line in SYSTEM: "Answer only what was asked. Do not mention the data below unless the question
  is about it."
  Research: a 2026 study of models under 7B parameters found that the mere presence of context, relevant
  or not, pulls answers off course. An earlier study found the effect grows with the amount of irrelevant
  text. The fix both point to is not to send it.
  RESULT (built 2026-10-14, committed in 1036282, NOT live)
    - jarvis.py: BLOCKS + wants(text, kind) pick the blocks; context(text, kind) and system_prompt(text,
      kind) take the question (None = everything, so any other caller is unchanged; turn() is the only
      caller). turn() passes the question plus the previous user line, so "and Friday?" keeps the calendar.
    - Sensor readings: all of them for a PC question or a briefing; otherwise only the Pending line, for
      time / setting words, under the "don't bring it up" label; otherwise none.
    - test_jarvis: block choice per question, profile in / out / capped, turn-level checks of the
      readings and the Pending line. Checks: OK under py -3 and venv (TZ=ABC+12); route check 0 real
      differences (one N14 line).
    - Limits: N17, N18. Rollback: git revert of the 6a commit.
    Live test (after copy to live): "Tell me a joke" (no alarms, calendar, PC numbers, profile); "What's
    on tomorrow?" then "and Friday?" (calendar both times); "Anything Friday?"; "How hot is my GPU?" (real
    numbers); with an alarm set, "did you set my alarm?" (the real time); "Good morning" (same briefing as

6b. One memory system (REWRITTEN 2026-10-14 by the user; replaces the old 6b and folds in 6d; ~190 lines)
  The user: keep talk longer, but sorted by topic and brought back only when the question is about that
  topic; old topics compacted into tiny facts, not deleted. Ideas from MemGPT/Letta (small working
  context, older talk moved out and summarised), Generative Agents (recall by relevance + recency), Mem0
  (add / update / ignore a fact, never stack contradictions), ChatGPT memory (saved facts you can list and
  delete, plus chat history used only when relevant). Ideas only; no new package.
  Four layers, one path:
    L1 ACTIVE  last 2 exchanges, 2 minutes (ACTIVE_TTL=120). Kept while the question links to them (a
               shared keyword, or a follow-up start: it, that, this, he, she, they, and, but, so, what
               about, how about, why, then, also; or 3 words or fewer). Unlinked, timed out or "new topic"
               -> filed into L2 and emptied. "Never mind" does NOT clear it (the user, 2026-10-14).
    L2 TOPICS  Data\jarvis_topics.json: a topic = keywords + its last 6 exchanges + last used. Filed talk
               joins the topic it shares 2 keywords (or 1 rare one) with, else starts a new one. Recalled
               only when the question shares keywords with it (a floor, so "time" or "day" alone never
               pulls one in); top 2, about 600 characters, under "From earlier talks (only if it helps)".
               Only chat exchanges, never commands or briefings. Digit runs of 6+ and password-like words
               stored as "[number]". JarvisRecall = on | off. "Forget what we said about X" reads back and
               waits for yes; "what do you remember about X" is answered by code. 7 days, 100 topics max.
    L3 GIST    after 7 days unused, or over 100 topics (oldest first): once a day while idle, the local
               model turns a topic into up to 3 tiny facts (JSON schema, like the AI fallback). CODE CHECKS
               every one: each number, name and content word must be in that topic's saved talk, else it is
               dropped. Model down or all dropped -> the topic stays and is tried tomorrow (removed without a
               gist after 3 failures, logged as a count). Data\jarvis_gist.txt, never expires; monthly,
               only if over 200 lines, the oldest lines sharing a topic are re-compacted under the same check.
    L4 FACTS   Data\jarvis_memory.txt: what YOU told him (6d below, unchanged as its spec). Word for word,
               never reworded or compacted by the model. Plus "what he actually did": code keeps the last 20
               actions it really ran (time, what, worked or not); questions about him get that list, or "You
               ran nothing in this session." This is how issue 28 is met by memory, without cutting his
               sentences.
  Prompt per turn: L1 (if linked), matching L4 facts (5 at most), at most 2 L2 topics + matching L3 lines,
  the action list only for questions about him. Nothing matches -> nothing is added.
  Order: 6a -> M1 (L1) -> M1b (last results) -> M2 (L2) -> Checkpoint A -> M4a (6d) -> M4b (actions) -> M3 (L3)
         -> Checkpoint B -> 6j (route log) -> ONE live copy + live test of all of Phase 6 -> 6f / 6i.
         (6e and 6g moved to PHASE 10 on 2026-10-14: everything about the voice.)
  Live tests (the user, 2026-10-14): ALL at the end of Phase 6. Checkpoints A and B are commit points only;
  their live checks join the end-of-phase test. Task breakdown: tasks/plan.md, tasks/todo.md (T1-T14).
  Each slice: its own tests, test_jarvis OK (py -3 and venv), route check, commit and live copy on the
  user's word.

  M1. Active window (about 25 lines, turn() and fixed())

    Why: "cancel the second one", "open it again", "when is that one" need something concrete to point

    - last = {"kind", "items", "when"}: set by code when he reads out a list (reminders / alarms, calendar
      events, to-dos, open apps) or runs an action on one target (the app opened, the alarm set).

      for kinds that already have a command (cancel a reminder, open / close an app); the result goes

  M2. Topic memory (about 60 lines)
    - Data\jarvis_topics.json, written .tmp then os.replace (as save_memory). Topic = {kw, talk:
      [[when, you, him], ...], used}. Lines capped at 200 characters, 6 exchanges per topic, 100 topics.
    - file_active(): chat exchanges only. Digit runs of 6+ and password-like words -> "[number]". Joins
      the topic sharing 2 keywords or 1 rare one (in only one topic), else a new topic.
    - Recall: score = shared keywords weighted by rarity + a small recency bonus, above a floor; best 2,
      about 600 characters; their "used" is updated.
    - JarvisRecall = on | off (off = L1 and L4 only).
    - Voice: "forget what we said about X" reads back the topics and waits for yes; "what do you remember
      about X" lists topics and gist by code.
    Tests: filing merges into the right topic; unrelated question recalls nothing, related one recalls the
    right topic; digits masked; JarvisRecall=off.

6d. Memory relook (about 35 lines)   FOLDED 2026-10-14 into 6b as layer L4 (M4a); this list is its spec
  Faults found in the code: all 50 facts go into every prompt; saving works only with the exact words
  "remember that" at the very start; forgetting matches exact words only; nothing stops two facts that
  contradict each other.
  Changes:
  - Saving accepts "remember that", "note that", "don't forget that", "keep in mind that" (after the name
    fix, so a misheard name no longer breaks it).
  - Only facts that share words with the question go into the prompt, 5 at most.
  - "What do you know about my dentist" lists the matching facts by code.
  - A new fact about the same thing as an old one asks: "Replace 'dentist on Fridays'?"
  - "Forget ..." reads back what it will remove and waits for yes when more than one fact matches.
  - The model can still never write to memory. Only a spoken command does.
  - Limits stay: 50 facts, 200 characters each, plain text in Data\jarvis_memory.txt, ignored by git.
  ANSWERED by the user on 2026-10-13. All four bothered them:
    1. He did not save what was said      -> the wider save phrases above, after the name fix (1a), and
                                             the "Noted: ..." read-back so a miss is heard at once.
    2. He brings facts up unasked         -> only matching facts in the prompt (above) plus the 6a line.
    3. He forgets or changes facts        -> matching facts are placed in the prompt word for word under
                                             "Facts the user told you. Use them exactly." A question that
                                             matches one fact closely ("when is my dentist") is answered
                                             by code with the stored words, not by the model.
    4. He should learn by himself         -> NEW, about 25 lines. The rule stays that the model never
                                             writes to memory. Instead: when you state a lasting fact about
                                             yourself in a chat turn ("my sister's name is ...", "I'm
                                             allergic to ...", "I work at ..."), fixed patterns in code
                                             spot it and he asks after his answer: "Want me to remember
                                             that?" A yes saves your own words. No answer saves nothing.
                                             At most one offer per turn, none for a fact already stored,
                                             and never for anything that looks like a password or number
                                             string. Setting JarvisLearn = ask (default) | off.
                                             shortcut: patterns, not the model, pick the facts, so he will
                                             miss unusual wording. Let the model propose candidates (still
                                             behind the same yes) if the patterns miss too much.
  Tests for 3 and 4: a close question returns the stored words without the fake model; a fake sentence
  "my sister's name is Ana" leads to one offer and a save only after yes; a digit string is never offered.

6f. A stronger chat model (needs approval; a download only if the chosen model is not installed)
  - With 12 GB on the RTX an 8B model fits. The code's own default is llama3.1:8b; the live setting is
    llama3.2:3b.
  - Compare the models that are already installed with a fixed list of 20 questions: does it mention
    unasked data, does it claim actions, does it keep to two sentences, seconds to the first word.
  - Choose by the result. Never the first model in the list. Log the chosen model and the reason.
  - PROPOSAL (2026-10-14, not approved yet): a bigger model only for hard questions. Do 6f first: moving
    from 3B to 8B may make it unneeded. 12 GB holds voice + small.en + an 8B (about 6 GB); a 14B (about
    9 GB) does not fit beside them, so each switch is several seconds of unload / load and may put part
    of a model on the CPU (what Phase 2 fixed). So: a spoken trigger found by code ("think hard about
    ...", "big brain mode"), a filler line while it loads, then back to the normal model. No automatic
    "is it complex?" guess unless the 6j log shows the trigger used often for the same kind of question.
    Setting JarvisModelBig (empty = off). About 20 lines. Numbers are estimates until measured.

6g. A smoother voice (added 2026-10-14 after the Phase 1 live test: "the voice doesn't sound very fluid yet")
    MOVED 2026-10-14 to PHASE 10 (everything about the voice, the user). Kept here as its spec.
  Not covered elsewhere: Phase 2e only makes the voice faster, 6e only changes the words.
  Measure first (no install): record one three-sentence answer and log, per sentence, the synthesis time,
  the gap between the end of one sentence and the start of the next, and the sentence length. Then fix
  only what the numbers show:
    - Gaps: speak_all() already synthesizes the next sentence while one plays. If gaps remain, the cause
      is a sentence that takes longer to make than the last one takes to play (then 2e's speed fixes it),
      or the model writing slower than he speaks (then start speaking after the first two sentences).
    - Choppy short pieces: join very short sentences (under about 6 words) before speaking them, so the
      voice gets enough text for natural intonation; never split mid-sentence.
    - A clipped start or end: a few ms fade in / out and no trimmed silence between sentences.
    - Pace: a JarvisVoiceSpeed setting (Kokoro speed, default 1.0, kept to 0.8-1.2).
  Judged by ear by the user; the logged gaps are the number that must go down. Best after Phase 2e.

    right now", never a guess. The full answer to "what is wrong" is Phase 9 (code finds, model words).
  - Widen 1d's action-claim check to self-activity claims: "I ran / was running / I tested / I
    simulated / I checked / I scanned ..." when the code ran nothing that turn -> the sentence is dropped
    and replaced by "I didn't run anything; that was made up." Logged with a count only.
