6i. GPU spikes to 100% on click, thinking and speaking (issue 27; added 2026-10-14)
  Likely causes (UNVERIFIED, from the Phase 2 design): click -> Whisper wakes on the RTX and listens;
  thinking -> Ollama generates on the RTX (near 100% while it writes is normal for a local model);
  speaking -> Kokoro on the GPU if 2e is in use, and the hologram's level updates. The question is
  whether a short spike is a problem (fan noise, heat, other apps or games stuttering) or just the card
  doing the work fast.
  Measure first (no change, no install): log GPU % and VRAM once a second with nvidia-smi during 5 click
  turns, 5 wake-word turns and idle, marked with the state (listening / thinking / speaking / idle).
  Report: peak, how long it stays high, and what is still loaded at idle. Also check the HUD's own GPU
  use with JARVIS off, so the skin's animation is not blamed on him.
  Then fix only what the numbers show, smallest first:
    - High while idle or listening: Whisper or the model staying busy -> find the loop.
    - Long high after he stops: shorter Ollama keep_alive or unload after the follow-up window.
    - Spikes that bother the user: a setting JarvisGPULimit = off (default) | gentle, where gentle uses
      the smaller model / fewer GPU layers (num_gpu) and keeps the voice on the CPU (slower replies).
  Judged by the user (does it bother you: noise, heat, stutter?) and by the logged numbers.
  Ties to Phase 2 (GPU first) and Phase 9 (he can explain a spike from real readings, see 6h).

6j. Which path answered, and how long it took (added 2026-10-14 from idea #1 of the suggestions file;
    about 10 lines; build before 6f / 6i, and before 6g in Phase 10)
  The suggestion was a central "priority router". route() already is one: fixed checks in a fixed order
  (guard, undo, never mind, fixed questions, reminders, actions, chat), with interpret() only for a missed
  command. Not taken: a new request_router.py, confidence scores, semantic matching. Taken: the log.
  - One line per turn at the end of turn(): "route: <kind> [<action>] via rule | ai-reread | model,
    total <s>s". No text of what was said, no targets (privacy, as the other log lines).
  - The timing lines that exist (click -> listening, end of speech -> text, -> reply start, -> reply done)
    stay; this adds the path and the total for fixed and action turns too, which today log no total.
  - Use: 6f (model compare), 6i, 6g (Phase 10) and the 6f big-model proposal measure from these lines; the user
    pastes them, Claude never reads Data\.
  Tests: a fixed command, an action and a chat turn each log one route line with the right path; the line
  has no words from the question.

Tests (fakes)
  - "what's 12 times 9" builds a prompt with no calendar, mail or to-do text.
  - "what's on today" has the calendar and nothing else extra.
  - History holds 4 messages and is gone after the fake 2 minutes.
  - A serious sentence gets the proper line even under playful.
  - Fact retrieval picks the right facts, 5 at most. A contradicting fact asks first.
  - (Phase 10) The cleaner output has no symbols.
  - (Phase 10) 6g: very short sentences are joined before the voice; a sentence is never split; the speed setting
    stays inside 0.8-1.2.

Your live test after Phase 6
  1. Ask ten everyday questions with nothing to do with school or mail. Count how often he mentions your
     schedule, mail or to-dos. Target: zero.
  2. "What's on today?" He does tell you the calendar.
  3. (Phase 10) Talk about something serious. No jokes.
  4. Ask about A, wait three minutes, ask about B. He does not bring A back. Then ask about A again:
     he recalls the earlier talk about A.
  5. "Remember that ..." in three different wordings, then ask about it tomorrow.
  6. (Phase 10) "Who made you?" Should name the user (with his infomation, calling me senai sorsa).
  7. (Phase 10) Under playful, ask "why is the GPU at 100%?" and a factual question. He answers from real
     numbers or says he can't see it; no made-up activity.
  8. (6i) Watch Task Manager > GPU during a few turns and compare with the 6i measurements.

FIX ROUND 5   BEFORE PHASE 7: HE WAKES ON NOISE   (added 2026-10-14 from the user's feature list)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Order: after the PHASE 6 LIVE TEST, before any Phase 7 work. Same rules as every phase (one at a time,
  tests, the user's live test, commit only on the user's word).
  The user's report: "it keeps picking up noise and misreading it as me speaking". The user's design:
  remove the bare "Jarvis" and the "Hey ... Jarvis" (pause) ways; make everything stricter: an easy check
  for "Hey" (0.1), then a harder check for "Jarvis" (0.35) that must come within 5 s; keep the normal
  "Hey Jarvis" check at about 0.35 as the backup.

  What the code does TODAY (read 2026-10-14, jarvis.py):
    - Only W0 exists: one hey_jarvis score per 80 ms >= JarvisWakeSensitivity (0.35 live), with the Silero
      speech filter at 0.5 (wake_loop ~l.4069-4089). R5-6 step 6b (W1-W3) was NEVER built; only the
      training (6a, train_wake + WAKE_STYLES) is. So the bare-"Jarvis" and pause ways cannot be the cause yet.
    - Other places that can turn noise into "you speaking": the follow-up window (no wake word needed,
      Fix Round 3), barge-in "voice" mode (any 0.56 s of speech while he talks), and Whisper phantom lines
      (partly filtered in Round 4).
  So the first step is to find WHICH path fires, not to guess.

  N0. Measure (no code; the user pastes lines, Claude never reads the log)
    For 2-3 false wakes, paste the lines around them: "wake word heard (0.xx)" or "barge-in (...)", the
    "transcript:" and "route:" lines. Plus the "wake: best 0.xx this minute" lines from 10 quiet minutes.
    This says: W0 at 0.35-0.5 (the threshold is too low for this room), the follow-up window, or barge-in.
    EVIDENCE ALREADY IN (chat history the user pasted 2026-10-14, live build 301c22f, the user never said
    "Hey Jarvis" in any of them):
      - 3 false wakes in about 45 minutes (at +0, +32 min, +45 min), all room speech ("Okay. So that's
        it. I'm going to go ahead...", "...in the mail for me.", "I'm still in four loop, so what's the
        name for it?"). None contains "Jarvis".
      - Each came 13-32 minutes after his last reply, so NOT the follow-up window (10 s) and NOT barge-in
        (only while he speaks). That leaves W0, the normal check at 0.35, firing on room talk. This
        supports raising the backup to 0.5 (N1). Still wanted: the "wake word heard (0.xx)" lines of these
        three, to see their scores.
      - The first transcript ends in "Okay." about 12 times: a Whisper repetition loop (N2).
      - He answered all three as if spoken to him, with jokes, and promised an action he cannot do (N3, and
        Phase 10).

  N1. R5-6 step 6b REDESIGNED (replaces W1-W3 above; the user's design + Claude's recommendation,
      approved by the user 2026-10-14)
    - Removed: W2 ("Hey ... Jarvis" as one style) and W3 (bare "Jarvis" wakes by itself).
    - "Hey" NEVER wakes him. It only makes him wait up to 5 s for "Jarvis". Nothing is recorded or
      transcribed during the wait.
    - The three trainings (WAKE_STYLES becomes "hey_jarvis", "hey", "jarvis"; the "hey_pause" style and
      any old wake_hey_pause.pkl are dropped, never loaded):
        1. "Hey Jarvis"  6 times -> wake_hey_jarvis.pkl  (YOUR voice saying the whole phrase)
        2. "Hey"         6 times -> wake_hey.pkl         (NEW: your "Hey" alone)
        3. "Jarvis"      6 times -> wake_jarvis.pkl      (your "Jarvis" alone)
        then 15 s of normal talk (the "not him" examples; "okay", "so", "all right" are good to include,
        since those woke him in the log).
    - Why "Hey" needs its own training: openWakeWord's shipped model only knows the whole phrase "Hey
      Jarvis" and scores mostly on the "...Jarvis" part, so its score is a poor "Hey" detector. Your own
      "Hey" check is. Training detail: the "hey" and "jarvis" clips are cut where the speech filter hears
      speech, not where the shipped score is above 0.1 (a bare "Hey" may never reach 0.1). The checks then
      run only on frames where the speech filter hears speech (silence costs nothing).
    - The ways to wake (any one):
        HJ. Your "Hey" check >= 0.5 (JarvisWakeEasy; easy) opens a 5 s wait (JarvisWakeGap = 5); inside it
            your "Jarvis" check >= 0.35 (JarvisWakeHard; the user's number, tuned from the training numbers)
            and speech heard -> he wakes. Log "wake word heard (0.xx, HJ)".
        HV. The shipped score >= 0.1 AND your "Hey Jarvis" check >= 0.5 (a soft "Hey Jarvis" in your
            voice). Log "(0.xx, HV)".
        W0. Backup: the shipped score >= 0.5 (JarvisWakeSensitivity 0.35 -> 0.5; the user sets it in the
            Control Center, or the default changes), as today otherwise. Log "(0.xx, W0)".
      Noise must now pass the shipped model at 0.5, or fool YOUR "Hey" and then YOUR "Jarvis" in order
      within 5 s, or the shipped model AND your "Hey Jarvis" check together.
    - Also stricter (cheap): S1. a score must hold 2 frames in a row (160 ms), not one. openWakeWord's
      predict() has this built in (patience); check the name in the installed 0.6.0 first.
    - Do these exist ready-made? The shipped "hey_jarvis" model does (W0, no training). The three checks
      do not: they are made from YOUR voice on purpose (the docs: tuned to one speaker and room). Training
      is built (6a, Control Center > WAKE WORD > TRAIN), about 2 minutes, once; again after a mic change.
      It reaches the live skin only with the copy to live. Train AFTER this round is built (the "hey"
      style is new).
    - Unverified until trained: whether your "Hey" check separates "Hey" from "okay" / "hey there"
      well, and the right JarvisWakeHard. If a style's check cannot be made, that way stays off and the
      others still work; with no .pkl at all only W0 (at 0.5) + S1 remain.
    Cost: logistic regression on features openWakeWord already computes, only while speech is heard; no
      Whisper, no GPU. Measure idle CPU before / after (6j-style lines).
    Tests (fakes): "Hey" alone never wakes; "Hey" then "Jarvis" within 5 s wakes; "Jarvis" after 6 s does
      not; "Jarvis" without "Hey" does not; HV at shipped 0.2 + check 0.6 wakes, check 0.3 does not; W0 at
      0.45 does not, at 0.5 for 2 frames does, for 1 frame does not; no .pkl -> W0 + S1 only; a
      wake_hey_pause.pkl present is never loaded; JarvisWakeEasy=0 -> HJ off.
    Live test: train; B1 (10x "Hey Jarvis" normal voice, aim 9+; 10x soft); "Hey ... (2 s) ... Jarvis"
      wakes; a bare "Jarvis" does not; B2 (30 min of normal room talk / video / music: 0 false wakes; the
      log above had 3 in 45 min).
    Rollback: git revert; JarvisWakeSensitivity back to 0.35; delete Data\wake_*.pkl.
    Risk: stricter = a soft "Hey Jarvis" from far away may need a second try. The training numbers and B1
      set the trade-off.

  N2. Whisper repetition loops ("Okay. Okay. Okay..." x12 in the log; about 10 lines in transcribe(), ~l.920)
    - faster-whisper gives each segment compression_ratio, avg_logprob and no_speech_prob. Drop a segment
      Whisper itself rates as a loop or as silence (its own defaults: compression ratio above 2.4; or
      no-speech above 0.6 with avg log-prob below -1). Then collapse any sentence repeated 3+ times in a
      row into one. condition_on_previous_text is already False.
    - Log "transcript: dropped (loop)" with counts only, never the words.
    - Check the attribute names in the installed faster-whisper first (Context7 + the venv).
    Tests: a fake segment list with a 2.6 ratio is dropped; "Okay. Okay. Okay. Okay." -> "Okay."; a normal
    sentence is unchanged.

  N3. Room talk answered as if it were for him (decide with the user before building)
    - After N1, far fewer of these reach him. For what still does: a transcript that is NOT a command,
      NOT a question to him and is long room talk (e.g. 25+ words, no "you" / his name) is handled one of
      two ways, the user picks: (a) dropped silently, log "transcript: dropped (not for me)"; or (b) one
      short "Were you talking to me?" with a 3 s follow-up.
    - Commands and questions are never dropped by this.
    Tests: the three log lines above -> (a) or (b); "what's the weather like today" -> answered.

REDESIGN   ONE FRONT DOOR, FOUR LANES   (approved 2026-10-14; solves Fix Round 6; done BEFORE Phase 7)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Full plan with sources: C:\Users\Senai\.claude\plans\linear-rolling-shell.md (kept outside the repo).
  Why: turn() is one long chain of special cases; nothing decides once what kind of turn it is, so noise,
  hallucinated "Thank you.", misheard commands and info questions all reach the chat model (L1-L10).
  Shape (all in jarvis.py, a new PIPELINE section, same process and threads):
    listen -> Whisper -> [H] hearing gate (drop noise / known Whisper phrases on weak evidence)
      -> [C] control check FIRST (stop, shut up, be quiet, never mind, yes / no, new topic, show log)
      -> [S] understand(): route() + fix_route() (0 ms) -> MiniLM embedding sorter (LANE_EXAMPLES, a
         few ms) -> ONE JSON model call only when unsure (interpret() extended: lane + corrected text,
         sees the last exchange, accepted only through the existing said-words checks) -> unsure: chat
      -> lane: command (code runs it, 0 model calls) | info (data only; no data = fixed reply, no model)
               | chat (talk; may only OFFER an action) | task (several steps; stub until Phase 7 / S2-2)
         each lane = its own prompt + context recipe + tools on the SAME loaded model, same num_ctx
      -> a lane may reroute once (never twice) -> [O] one output sink (pop-up types at speaking pace)
      -> [F] follow-up gate (chat / info only; JarvisFollowUpMax, the Control Center shows the same value)
  User decisions 2026-10-14:
    - Stop while speaking: Vosk keyword spotter (new dependency, installed only at slice F with approval)
      with NO echo: echo cancelling fed with his own played samples + ignore a stop word he is saying
      himself + live echo test (10 replies with "stop" on speakers: 0 false stops; 10 real stops: >= 9).
      No Windows wheel for an echo canceller -> stop and ask the user.
    - Model: qwen3 4B for all lanes (user pulls it; tag and think:false checked with Context7 first;
      measured against the current model on the phrase list before switching).
    - Rollout: replace directly, no old/new switch. Each slice its own commit; rollback = git revert.
  Slices (each: approval -> build -> tests -> user live test -> commit on the user's word):
    M0 baseline (CPU / RAM / VRAM, timing lines) + replay test of today's routes
    A  hearing gate + control check (L4, L9 empty wakes, L10 noise, L1 between replies)
    B  understand() + embedding sorter + last-exchange context (L2)
    C  lanes; info no-data reply (L10)
    D  reroute + follow-up gate + Control Center sync (L6, L7)
    E  output sink, typing at speaking pace (L8)
    F  stop while speaking: Vosk + echo cancelling (L1)
    G  wake: user first sets STRICTER 0.50, TRAIN, Whisper small.en; code only if still needed (L5, L9)
    H  clean-up of code the slices left unused; then re-run the Phase 6 checklist + F1-F7 (L3)
  Phases 7-10 and Stage 2 then build on lanes: P7 settings = command entries / task lane, P8 = [F],
  P9 = info source "self", P10 = per-lane prompts (hand-reviewed), S2-1 = this design, S2-2 = task tool.
  Known gaps (later): smart end-of-turn detection, speaker ID, hardware-grade echo cancelling.
  M0 BASELINE (measured 2026-10-14 evening, JARVIS pid started 18:49, idle, no Ollama model loaded):
    - GPU: RTX 5070 Laptop, 12231 MiB; 150 MiB in use, 0 % busy (JARVIS has a CUDA context; Whisper base.en).
    - JARVIS (pythonw): 1806 MB RAM; idle 5.0 % of ONE core over 10 s (20 logical cores). Ollama: 75 MB.
    - Per-turn timings: from the 6j lines in the user's next pasted log (Claude does not read Data\).
    - Route check (this session's scratchpad m0\check_route.py = the 65-phrase check + 10 FR6 phrases):
      HEAD vs working copy (FR5): 75 phrases, 0 differences; kinds: chat 43, act 16, remind 11, notes 2,
      say 2, fact 1.
    - FR6 phrases today: "about 20%", "thank you", "okay oh okay", "shut up", "be quiet", "that's enough",
      "stop", "were you talking to me", "do I have a meeting tonight" ALL route to chat, ask_ai False,
      room_talk False. (ENDERS only catches stop words inside a follow-up.) "set the volume to 20%" -> act
      volume. These are the "before" values for slices A-C.
  MODEL CHECK (2026-10-14): qwen3:4b-instruct-2507-q4_K_M pulled (2.5 GB; non-thinking build, accepts
    think:false). 3 test prompts vs llama3.2:3b: about the same speed (0.2-1.0 s), +600 MiB VRAM (3182 vs
    2594 MiB in use); read "About 20%." after a volume talk as a command (llama3.2: "remind"). JarvisModel
    NOT switched yet: the full phrase-list measurement is part of slice B.
  BRANCH IDEA (user, 2026-10-14: statement -> category -> subcategory -> task): evaluated.
    - Used by current assistants as a NAME structure (Alexa domain -> intent -> slots, Rasa retrieval
      intents, hierarchical semantic routers), but they classify in ONE step, not level by level.
    - Level by level multiplies the errors (3 levels at 95 % each = 86 %) and adds a delay per level when
      a model decides; with about 30 actions one flat step is more accurate and faster.
    - Adopted only as naming: every action gets a path lane.group.action (command.volume.set,
      info.pc.temps, chat, task.settings.multi). route() / the sorter / the JSON call still return the
      final action in one step. Costs nothing at run time; makes the Control Center list, logs and
      Stage 2 additions easier. Added in slice B (Decision.path) and C (LANES registry).
  SLICE A BUILT 2026-10-14 (uncommitted, waiting for the user's live test):
    - ENDERS now ends ANY turn quietly (was: follow-ups only) and also catches shut up, be quiet,
      that's enough, stop it / stop talking, shush, hush, and repeats ("Thank you. Thank you."). Runs
      after the offer yes / fact checks and before every route, so these never reach the model (L1, L4).
    - Nothing heard after the WAKE WORD ends quietly (L9); a click still gets "Sorry, I didn't catch
      that." once. turn(tier, follow, woke): the wake loop and barge_turn pass woke=True.
    - Not added (kept behaviour): bare "okay" / "yes" stay normal answers, because after a chat question
      they mean yes. "Okay oh okay" still goes to chat; slice B decides it.
    - Changed tests: a new-turn "Never mind." now ends quietly (was: "Okay, never mind.").
    - COURTESY (the user, 2026-10-14: "thank you" still gets "you're welcome"; its own category of brief,
      proper 1-3 word replies that add nothing): a table COURTESY checked just before ENDERS, from code,
      no model, no follow-up: thanks / thank you / cheers (also "okay thanks Jarvis", repeats) ->
      "You're welcome."; bye / goodbye / see you -> "Goodbye."; good night -> "Good night."; sorry / my
      bad -> "No problem.". "No thank you" stays silent (a decline). One guard for L4: a "Thank you."
      right after a WAKE that Whisper was unsure of (heard_score < -1) is treated as noise: silence.
      Words outside the table ("thanks for the help with my essay") go on as before. Lane name later:
      courtesy (part of [C], not a model lane).
      Greetings added (the user, 2026-10-14): hello / hi / hey / hiya / howdy (+ there, + Jarvis) ->

    - understand(text, model, quiet, room) -> Decision(kind, action, target, prompt, via). It holds what

    - CHANGED FROM THE PLAN: Decision.path and the embedding sorter move to slice C. Without lanes they

    - Lanes: lane_of() sorts each turn into command / info / chat. info = notes, apps, brief, or a chat

    1. Settings.inc: the repo is public and the committed copy held 4 personal paths (LltPath,
       ObsidianTodo, ObsidianVault, JarvisVault). The next commit has them blank (staged in the index

       it. Blank is supported: status.ps1 Resolve-Llt searches the usual folders, the to-do / vault rows
       say "not set", ObsidianVault is read by nothing. TRAP: before a slice changes Settings.inc on
       purpose, run git update-index --no-skip-worktree on it, and stage the change without the 4 values.

    2. N13 FIXED: the alarm tests pin the clock to 3 AM today (still ticking) and then restore it. New
       test: "set an alarm for 2 am" at 3 AM says "2:00 AM tomorrow" (a time already gone rolls to the
       next day; when() did that already). No TZ needed any more.
    3. SLEEP button (Control Center, SYSTEM): rundll32 cannot pass SetSuspendState's arguments, so it
       could hibernate. Now ShipCore.exe sleep calls SetSuspendState(false, false, false). core.ps1
       rebuilds ShipCore.exe on the next skin load (ShipCore.cs is newer). control.lua: core() moved
       above rowsSystem() so the row can use it. Row text "Sleep the PC".
    4. KOKORO_VOICES deleted (never read).
    5. Daylight saving: when() rolled a gone time to tomorrow by adding 24 hours, so on a clock-change
       night "7 AM" became 6 or 8 AM. Now it adds one calendar day (timedelta(days=1) on the local
       datetime). Checked under TZ=CST6CDT on both change nights: old 06:00 / 08:00, new 07:00 / 07:00.
       Repeating alarms already used timedelta.

REFACTOR   SPLIT jarvis.py INTO MODULES (added 2026-10-15; runs BEFORE PHASE 7, the user's order)
  WHY: jarvis.py is one ~4700-line file. Phases 7-10 and STAGE 2 each touch it; smaller modules with
  clear owners make each later change smaller and safer. No new features, no new dependencies, no UI
  change. Behaviour must stay the same; the live tests stay where they are (after Phase 7).
  ADJUSTMENTS (the user, 2026-10-15):
    1. The refactor runs before Phase 7 (the user overrode Claude's "after" suggestion).
    2. Tests are made split-safe FIRST (R1), so every later step is checked by the same tests.
    3. jarvis.py stays the entry point and the integrator: the skin still runs
       pythonw "<RES>Jarvis\jarvis.py" "<RES>". Leaves move out one at a time, easiest first, into
       Jarvis\jarvislib\<name>.py; jarvis.py imports them back (from jarvislib.x import ...), so every
       old name still works as jarvis.<name> (test_jarvis.py, check_voice.py, testkoroko.py).
    4. The knot (turn / exchange / history / gist / did-undo / wake_loop / main / stop key) goes LAST
       and is split only where the owner of the state is clear; whatever is not clear stays in jarvis.py.
    5. Known weaknesses are LOGGED below, NOT fixed in the refactor.
  CONTRACTS THAT MUST NOT CHANGE (from the Phase 1 audit, 2026-10-15):
    - Launch line above; the venv / JarvisPython / pyw interpreter order in jarvis.lua; RES = argv[1].
    - Single instance: Data\jarvis.lock (msvcrt). Quit: QUIT in jarvis_cmd.txt or ~30 s of missed checks.
    - HUD -> Python: jarvis_cmd.txt "WORD <ts> [arg]" (WAKE, CANCEL, FORGET, REINDEX, TRAINWAKE, QUIT),
      mtime-polled, ignored when older than 30 s; jarvis_env.txt MUTED 0|1.
    - Python -> HUD: jarvis.txt (T/STATE/LEVEL/SAY/ERR/POP, atomic, 2 s heartbeat; >8 s = offline),
      jarvis_log.txt, jarvis_live.txt, jarvis.log, jarvis_models.txt, jarvis_mics.txt, jarvis_duck.txt.
    - Python-owned data files and Settings.inc keys keep their names and formats.
    - route() precedence; confirmation (exact yes tied to one pending action, expiry), truth check
      (no_claims), did/undo; the "route: ..." line; voice and audio lifecycle; logs stay private.
  HOW EACH STEP IS DONE AND CHECKED
    - A move is verbatim (code and the comments above it), done by a script that refuses when moved
      code would need a name still in jarvis.py (no upward imports) or a rebound global ("global X")
      that jarvis.py still reads. Moved modules import only the std library and earlier jarvislib modules.
    - Checks after EVERY step, on a scratch copy (never in the repo; importing writes Data\):
      py_compile of jarvis.py + jarvislib\*.py; test_jarvis.py -> "test_jarvis: OK"; check_route.py
      HEAD-before-refactor vs now -> no change except the known time-stamp noise line; no control chars.
    - One commit per step (files staged by name). ROLLBACK of a step: git revert <its commit>.
    - Moving code into a package changes one path: RES's default (used only without argv) becomes
      the folder two levels above jarvislib\. Every other path is RES-based and does not move.
  STEPS (in order; each one a commit)
    R1  test_jarvis.py: "j" becomes a proxy; "j.X = fake" sets X in jarvis AND in every jarvislib module
        bound to the same object, so the 258 fakes keep working after a move. DONE 2026-10-15.
    R2  base: paths, settings, log, State / jarvis.txt writer, lock, convo_add / live.
    R3+ leaves, one per commit, easiest first: llm (HTTP / chat), AI interpret, hearing (whisper / GPU /
        transcribe), mic / duck / listen, speech (TTS / stop words / fillers), notes + chroma, actions
        (+ YES / is_yes / confirmed), calc / convert / facts, memory, reminders / alarms, router.
        A step whose leaf turns out to depend on the knot is skipped and stays in jarvis.py (noted here).

  R-END  DONE 2026-10-15: where things live now, and where each later item goes.

    RULES FOR NEW CODE (Phases 7-10, STAGE 2)
      - A new subsystem gets its own jarvislib module; jarvis.py imports it and wires it in. A module
        never imports jarvis.py (if it needs something from the knot, the call stays in jarvis.py).
      - Adding to an existing module: put it in the module that owns that state (table above).
      - test_jarvis.py: keep writing "j.X = fake"; the proxy reaches every jarvislib module by itself.
      - Change a "global X" in a module only inside that module; elsewhere use module.X (see unload_idle).
      - Checks per step as in "HOW EACH STEP IS DONE AND CHECKED" above (check_route compares HEAD
        before the step against the working tree).

    - Headphones: NOT typed into a setting by hand. A Control Center row (JARVIS SETTINGS, 7e) lists the
      PAIRED audio devices; for each the user sets two things: the Bluetooth name (picked from the list
      Windows gives) and the name the user will call it ("headphones", "airpods", any words). Several
      devices allowed. "Connect my airpods" matches the user's own name first, then the Bluetooth name
      (best_app() style fuzzy match). Stored as JarvisBtNames = "<bt name>=<spoken name>;..." in
      Settings.inc (device names only, not logged).
    - Brightness can go to 0 percent (no floor).
    - Dark / light mode switches BOTH the apps and Windows itself (both registry values).
    - Undo covers every new change to a number or an on/off switch: brightness, mic level, mic mute,
      Bluetooth on/off and connect/disconnect, dark mode, power mode, his own stealth / mute. "Undo that"
      puts the previous value back (read from Windows before the change), the same can_undo / undo_last
      path the alarms use. Actions that cannot be undone (recycle bin, screenshot) say so.
    - Power mode: he may NEVER pick a mode that stops his own functions. Allowed: the Windows modes that
      keep the CPU, GPU, microphone and network running (Best performance, Balanced). Battery saver /
      a plan that turns the GPU or network off is refused with the reason. The list of allowed plan ids is
      read from the machine once and checked by code, never by the model.
    - He can also OPEN: Wi-Fi settings, apps (Installed apps / Startup apps pages), storage settings
      (fixed ms-settings: links in the settings-pages table).
    - Yes words: the SAME confirmation as every other command - yes, go ahead, continue, proceed, and
      anything meaning yes. One shared YES set (jarvis.py l.1779). "continue" and "carry on" are missing
      from it today and are added there (so every confirmation gains them, not just Phase 7).
    - Yes window: as the existing confirmations (unchanged).

Fixes:  issue 13 ("or anything else")
        issue 20 (microphone settings, Bluetooth settings, "connect to ___")
        issue 22 (airplane mode and other easy settings, section 7d)
Depends on Phase 1 (name fix, near-miss catch, the truth rule) and Phase 3 (hearing).

Rules for every new action
  - It is a new fixed entry in ACTIONS. Nothing else can run. No shell text is ever built from speech.
  - He says what Windows reports afterwards, never what was asked for.
  - Anything that can cut you off (Bluetooth off, Wi-Fi off, microphone mute) asks for a yes first.
  - Device names are matched against the list Windows gives. They are not written to the log.
  - Tests use fakes: no real device is connected, muted or switched during a test.

7-0. One action table (added 2026-10-14 from idea #9 of the suggestions file; build first in Phase 7;
     needs the user's approval of the exact layout before building)
  Why here: Phase 7 adds about 20 new actions. Today an action is spread over three places (a regex in
  route(), an entry in ACTIONS, a confirmation / reply in turn()). One table entry per action makes each
  new one a single, testable block.
  - ACTIONS entries become small records: name, the phrases (regex) that call it, risk (read | change |
    ask-first: ask-first always gets the yes question), run(target), check(target) that reads Windows
    back (what he says afterwards, as the Phase 7 rule above), and one line of description.
  - The AI_SCHEMA list of actions interpret() may choose is built from the same table, so the model can
    only name an action that exists, as today.
  - NOT taken from the suggestion: discovering tools by loading every Python file in a folder (any file
    dropped there would run). The table is one fixed list in jarvis.py.
  - Existing actions move one at a time, each with the route check showing 0 differences and test_jarvis
    OK; the new Phase 7 actions are written in the new form from the start. If a move changes any route,
    it stops there.
  - Risk is decided by code, never by the model; DANGER and the 1d claim check stay as they are.
  Tests: every table entry has a check(); an ask-first entry always asks; a phrase reaches the same
  action as before the move (route check); interpret() can't return a name missing from the table.

7a. Microphone (about 30 lines, no new package)
    Say                                         Result
    ------------------------------------------  ----------------------------------------------------
    "mute the microphone" / "mute my mic"       asks first, because he cannot hear "unmute" afterwards.
                                                A click on the hologram unmutes it again.
    "unmute the microphone"                     unmutes (only useful from a click-started turn)
    "set the microphone to 80 percent"          sets the input level
    "turn the microphone up / down a bit"       10 steps
    "which microphone are you using"            names the device
    "use the headset microphone"                sets JarvisMic (3d). JARVIS's own choice; Windows' default
                                                is not changed.
    "be more sensitive" / "less sensitive"      JarvisWakeSensitivity one step
    "wait longer" / "answer faster"             JarvisSilence one step
  How: speakers() (l.1197) already talks to Windows Core Audio. The capture device is the same call with
  the data-flow argument 1 instead of 0. One shared function, one extra argument.

7b. Bluetooth (about 60 lines; the connect step needs an experiment first)
    Say                                         Result
    ------------------------------------------  ----------------------------------------------------
    "turn Bluetooth on"                         switches the radio on
    "turn Bluetooth off"                        asks first (it disconnects a Bluetooth mouse or headset)
    "what's connected" / "Bluetooth devices"    reads the connected devices
    "connect to <name>"                         finds the best match among PAIRED devices and tries to
                                                connect. Then checks the real state and says "Connected
                                                to ..." or "I couldn't connect to ...".
    "disconnect from <name>"                    the same, the other way
    "pair a new device"                         opens the Windows Bluetooth page. Pairing needs your
                                                consent on screen, so it is never done by voice.
  How, cheapest first:
    - Radio on / off and the device list: Windows' own interfaces called from one fixed PowerShell line,
      or through ctypes. No new package.
    - Matching the spoken name: the same fuzzy scoring best_app() uses. Two close matches: he asks which.
    - Connecting: Windows has no simple public command for "connect this paired device". For AUDIO devices
      (headphones, speakers) the method Windows' own Connect button uses can be called through the same
      COM helper the volume code uses. This is the experiment: try it on one paired headset, with the
      user present, before it is built in.
    - If it cannot connect that way: he opens the Windows quick-connect panel and says "I opened the list,
      pick it there." He never says "connected" unless Windows reports it.
    - "Whatever he can": paired audio devices first. A device that is off, out of range or never paired
      gets the plain answer.

  DECIDED by the user on 2026-10-13. Build now: power mode, the small actions (screenshot, show desktop,

  NOT now: airplane mode, mobile hotspot, battery saver, screen off, second screen, refresh rate. They
  stay in the table for later; do not build them unless the user asks again.

Tests (fakes)
  - "airplane mode off" turns back on only the radios the fake recorded as turned off by him.
  - Recycle bin, refresh rate and airplane mode on do nothing without a yes.
  - Each phrase routes to the right action with the right argument.
  - The microphone level uses the capture device, not the speakers.

    "couldn't connect" and never "connected".
  - Bluetooth off, Wi-Fi off and microphone mute do nothing without a yes.
  - No test touches a real device.

Your live test after Phase 7 (one setting at a time)
  1. "Mute the microphone." -> "Yes." Then check Windows sound settings. Click the hologram to undo.
  2. "Set the microphone to 70 percent." Check the level in Windows.
  3. "Turn Bluetooth off." -> "Yes." Then "turn Bluetooth on." (with a wired or built-in keyboard at hand)
  4. Headphones switched on: "connect to <their name>". Sound moves to them, or he says he could not.
  5. "Connect to banana." He says he has no such device.
  6. "Brightness to 40."
  7. "Airplane mode on." -> "Yes." Wi-Fi and Bluetooth go off. "Airplane mode off." Both come back.
  8. "Extend the screen" with a second monitor attached. "Take a screenshot." Check the Pictures folder.

Unverified until the live run: every one of these, because fakes cannot connect a real device.
Risk: muting the microphone or turning Bluetooth off can cut you off from him. That is why both ask first
and why the click always works.
Rollback: revert the commit. Each action is undone by its opposite or in Windows settings.

  PART 1b  Phase 7 as built 2026-10-15 (branch chore/jarvis-baseline-and-performance, not pushed, not live).
           Only fakes were run so far; every real WRITE below is untested.
    1b-1. Action table: ask-first rows (mic mute, Bluetooth off, power mode, recycle bin) do nothing until you
          say yes; a bare "yes" with nothing pending does nothing (7-0).
    1b-2. "Go to sleep" when sleep is off: he says the screen was turned off instead (7-L1).
    1b-3. Mic: "mute the microphone" asks first, then mutes; "unmute the mic"; "mic up a bit" (+10 percent);
          "set the mic to 40"; "undo". Click the orb while the mic is muted: it unmutes (click path untested).
    1b-4. Brightness: "brightness 50", "a bit brighter" (+10), "0" is allowed, "above 100" gets an answer,
          "undo"; the level he says is the one Windows reports.
    1b-5. "Dark mode" / "light mode" / "turn off dark mode": apps AND Windows change; he reads the result back; "undo".
    1b-6. Settings pages: "open sound settings", "night light" / "turn on focus" (he says Windows has no switch and
          opens the page), "what starts with windows" (opens Startup apps), "are there updates".
    1b-7. "Take a screenshot": a new file in Pictures\Screenshots, else he says it failed. "Show the desktop".
    1b-8. "Empty the recycle bin": asks first, says it cannot be undone, then empties it.
    1b-9. Power mode: "balanced mode" (asks, reads back, undo). "Best performance" says the PC has no such plan
          if only Balanced is listed. "Power saver" is refused with a reason.
    1b-10. Bluetooth: "turn bluetooth on" / "turn bluetooth off" (off asks first); he says the state Windows
          reports; "undo". "Pair a new device" opens the Bluetooth page. "Connect my headphones" opens the page and
          says he cannot connect by voice yet.
    1b-11. "What's the weather" / news / prices: "I can't look that up yet." (7-A9), no model guess.
    1b-12. "Open Chrome" (or any app already running): the existing window comes to the front, no second copy;
          an app not running starts as before (7-A8).
    1b-13. Stealth: "stealth mode" -> he asks -> "yes" -> the HUD goes to STEALTH and he answers in the pop-up only.
          He cannot undo it by voice; leave it in the Control Center. "Mute yourself" = sound mute, not stealth.
    1b-14. Microphone: "which microphone are you using" -> he names the current one. "Use the headset microphone"
          -> asks, "yes" -> "Listening through ... It applies the next time I listen." The Control Center
          MICROPHONE row shows the new name after a moment. "Undo" -> back to the old one. "Use the webcam
          microphone" (no match) -> he says he cannot find it. "Use the default mic" -> Windows default.
    1b-15. Bluetooth audio (radio ON, headset paired): "which bluetooth devices are connected" -> lists them.
          "Connect my headphones" -> asks, "yes" -> "Connected to X." (check it really connects). "Undo" ->
          disconnects. "Disconnect the headphones" -> asks, yes, check. Two matching devices -> he lists them.
          Radio OFF -> "turn it on first". Check the services are enabled again after a disconnect + connect.
    1b-16. Data\jarvis_log-YYYY-MM.txt exists and grows with each exchange; "forget" clears only jarvis_log.txt.
    1b-17. Control Center JARVIS tab: three headings; every old row is still there; scroll works; buttons work.
    1b-18. Fresh install: Setup-Jarvis.ps1 leaves JarvisPython blank, writes a relative JarvisVoice, voice bm_lewis.
    1b-19. Control Center JARVIS tab, new rows: AI ROUTER / GPU / LEARN / RECALL toggles change and survive RELOAD;
          FIRST WORDS, IDLE UNLOAD, WAKE STRICT -/+ stay inside their limits; BLUETOOTH NAMES lists the paired
          names after one "connect my headphones" (needs Data\jarvis_bt.txt); REMEMBERED FACTS shows the count, OPEN
          shows the file, FORGET ALL needs a second click and then the file is empty (this one is not undoable).

Fixes:  issue 17 (follow-up only within 3 seconds, or "Jarvis ..." within 10 seconds)
        issue 1  ("Jarvis online" off, startup noises much quieter)
Small and independent: it can be done at any point.

8a. Follow-up windows (about 20 lines, in turn() and _listen())
  - After a spoken answer the microphone stays open for 10 s in total.
  - Speech that STARTS within 3 s is a follow-up, as today.
  - Speech that starts between 3 and 10 s counts only if it begins with his name (alias-aware, from 1a).
    Anything else is dropped and not logged.
  - Nothing after 10 s. No cap on follow-ups in a row (user, 2026-10-14; built in R5-3).
  - Settings JarvisFollowWindow=3 and JarvisFollowNameWindow=10. JarvisFollowUp=0 still turns it all off.
  - The hologram shows "listening" for the first 3 s, then a dimmer "standby" until 10 s.
  - For comparison: Alexa keeps listening about 5 s and Google about 8 s, with no name rule.
  - Stated plainly: room speech in seconds 3 to 10 is transcribed to look for the name, then dropped.

8b. Sounds (about 20 lines)
  - Phase 0 says where "Jarvis online" lives. If it is inside a wav file, that sound is switched off by
    default and the file stays in the folder.
  - The chime is played through the daemon's own sound path with a new setting JarvisChimeVolume
    (default 25 percent) instead of winsound, which has no volume control.
  - The boot sound is played by Rainmeter itself, which has no volume control either. Smallest fix: a
    quieter copy of the wav, made once by a small script, with the original kept beside it.
  - BootSound=0 turns the boot sound off completely.

8c. Quiet during class (added 2026-10-14 from the user's feature list; about 25 lines)
  - The HUD already writes Data\calendar.txt every 10 min (E <start>|<end>|<allday>|<label>|<title>) and
    jarvis.py already reads it (l.352). No new calendar plugin or login is needed.
  - Setting JarvisQuietCalendar = the label of the calendar that holds the classes (e.g. "School"); empty =
    off. While a timed (not all-day) event of that calendar is running, he is QUIET: he still wakes and
    works, but every reply is the HUD pop-up only, as in STEALTH. No sound, no chime.
  - "You can talk" / "speak normally" (or a click on a Control Center button) ends QUIET for that event
    only. The next class starts quiet again.
  - Alarms during QUIET: pop-up only (ASK THE USER before building: is a silent alarm in class OK?).
  - Event titles are not logged and not spoken; the log says "quiet: class until 10:45".
  Tests: a fake calendar.txt with an event now -> quiet; an all-day event -> not; "you can talk" -> speaks
  until the next event; empty setting -> never quiet.

8d. Type to him (added 2026-10-14 from the user's feature list; about 40 lines)
  - A text box (Rainmeter's built-in InputText plugin) on the hologram's right-click or in the Control
    Center. Enter writes "ASK <unix> <text>" to Data\jarvis_cmd.txt, the same file a click already uses.
  - The daemon runs that text through the SAME turn as speech (route(), confirmations, the claim check),
    with no Whisper and no listening. The reply is shown as text, and spoken unless muted / QUIET.
  - Typed text is treated like spoken text: untrusted. Cut at 500 characters; one line; a "yes" for a
    confirmation must come from the same channel (typed question -> typed yes, or a click).
  Tests: an ASK line reaches route() unchanged; a too-long line is cut; ASK while busy is refused with
  "One moment, I'm busy." (as a click is).

8e. "Think hard about this" (added 2026-10-14; about 15 lines)
  - A question that begins or ends with "think hard / think really hard (about this)" goes to a bigger
    model in Ollama (setting JarvisModelDeep, empty = off; e.g. a 14B that the user has pulled). pick_model()
    gets one more tier. He says "Thinking it over." first. Everything else keeps today's model.
  - The deep model is loaded only for that question and unloaded after (Ollama keep_alive 0), so it does
    not hold the graphics card. Measure first: its load time and whether it fits next to Whisper on this GPU.
  Tests: the phrase picks the deep tier and is stripped from the question; no setting -> normal model.

Tests (fakes)
  - Speech at 2 s is taken. Speech at 5 s without the name is dropped; with the name it is taken.
  - Nothing after 10 s. Many in a row, no cap (R5-3).
  - The chime samples are scaled by the setting.

Your live test after Phase 8
  1. Ask something. Answer within 3 seconds without his name: he takes it.
  2. Ask something. Wait 5 seconds, say a sentence without his name: he ignores it.
  3. Ask something. Wait 5 seconds, say "Jarvis, and tomorrow?": he takes it.
  4. Wait 12 seconds, say "Jarvis, hello": nothing (the wake word is needed again).
  5. Restart JARVIS and Rainmeter: no "Jarvis online", and the sounds are quiet.

Rollback: the settings. The original wav files stay in the folder.

    9-L1 THE HUD'S ERR STAYS UP (audit item 12)
         base.State's ERR stays until the next turn when a problem had no pop-up. Fix: keep the time of
         the error; clear ERR after 60 s or on the next good turn; "what went wrong?" (9b) answers from
         the last error and its time, so the HUD does not have to hold it. Test: ERR is gone after the
         time limit; the diagnosis answer still names it.
    9-L2 THE SLEEP ERROR NUMBER IN THE LOG MAY BE WRONG (audit item 15)   DONE 2026-10-15 (review BUG-04, 2e33b08)
         act_sleep logs ctypes.GetLastError() through ctypes.windll, which does not keep the error.
         Fix: ctypes.WinDLL("powrprof", use_last_error=True) and ctypes.get_last_error(). Diagnosis
         reads this log, so it should be true. Test with a fake DLL.
    9-L3 THE DAILY GIST ADD IS AN APPEND (audit item 14, W-c)
         memory.compact appends to jarvis_gist.txt; a crash mid-write can leave one cut line, which
         load_gist then reads as a short fact. Fix: write the whole file with base.write_atomic (load,
         add, write; the file is small), like every other store. Test: the save goes through write_atomic.
         (The Gemini audit lists the same fix as P0-12; it is built in AUDIT-0, so this one is then DONE.)
  FROM THE GEMINI AUDIT (2026-10-15, accepted by the user)
    9-A1 (P1-17) = 9-L1 above: ERR cleared after 60 s or on the next good turn; "what went wrong?" answers
         from the stored error and its time. Merged, not new.
    9-A2 (P1-27) Pre-made fillers: a "diag" filler ("Checking the system.", already in 9a step 2) is added to
         FILLERS with this phase. The "thinking" filler goes with S2-16 (deep model).
    9-A3 (P1-25) If foreground-app info is wanted for a diagnosis answer, read it once on demand (no polling).
         The polling version is S2-17.

Fixes:  issue 21 (system logs, Task Manager information, which processes overload the system, "I could
        just ask him what the issue is")
Depends on: Phase 1 (truth rule, near-miss catch) and Phase 6a (a data block is sent only when the
question needs it). Best after Phase 2, because a diagnosis answer is longer and needs the speed.
Everything in this phase is READ-ONLY. It adds no action that changes the system.

What exists today: sensors() (l.280) gives total CPU load, temperatures from the HUD, and GPU load and
memory from nvidia-smi. Nothing per process, no logs, no disk, no network check.
The venv has NO psutil, pynvml, wmi or pywin32 (checked 2026-10-13).

9a. The design: code finds, the model words
  1. A trouble question is recognised by route(): "why is my PC slow", "what's using my CPU", "why is the
     fan loud", "what crashed", "is something wrong", "my Wi-Fi is slow", "no sound", "what's wrong".
  2. Code takes ONE snapshot (9b), on demand only. Nothing is polled in the background, so idle cost is 0.
     While it runs he says a pre-made filler ("Checking the system.") like the notes search does.
  3. Code turns the snapshot into FINDINGS with fixed rules (9d), ranked by severity.
  4. Simple questions are answered by code alone: "Chrome is using 71 percent of the CPU."
  5. For "what's wrong" the findings, never the raw data, go to the model with one instruction: explain
     these in two sentences, most serious first, invent nothing. No finding: the fixed line
     "I can't see anything wrong: CPU 12 percent, memory 48 percent, disk fine, no errors today."
  Why: a 3B model given a raw process list or raw log lines invents causes. Rules cannot.

9b. The snapshot (about 60 lines of Python plus one fixed script; about 1 to 2 seconds)
    Source                        What is read                                  Windows source
    ----------------------------  --------------------------------------------  ---------------------------
    Processes (Task Manager)      top 5 by CPU, by memory, by disk, by GPU;     performance counters
                                  names and numbers only. Many processes of      (Process, GPU Engine,
                                  one app are added up ("Chrome, 31 processes").  GPU Process Memory)
    Totals                        CPU, memory used and committed, page file      the same counters
    Disks                         free space per drive, busy time                counters, shutil
    Heat and power                temperatures, fan, battery level, charging,    sensors() today; battery
                                  battery wear, throttling                       from Windows
    Network                       connected or not, Wi-Fi signal, time to the    netsh wlan show interfaces,
                                  router and to one public address               two pings
    Devices                       devices Windows marks as faulty (count, kind)  the device list
    Windows                       time since last restart, restart pending,      Windows
                                  start-up app count
    JARVIS himself                his own problem lines (fixed texts), which     memory, not the log file
                                  parts are on the GPU, why the wake word is off

  How it is fetched, to be decided by a small timing experiment before building:
    A. One fixed PowerShell script in the Jarvis folder (diag.ps1) that prints JSON. No new package.
       Cost: PowerShell takes about a second to start. Recommended first.
    B. The psutil package (about 1 MB): faster and less code for processes, but it does not give per-process
       GPU use or the event log, so A is needed anyway. Only with approval.
  No spoken text is ever passed to the script. Its only arguments are fixed words chosen by code
  ("processes", "logs", "network"). Exit code and JSON are checked; bad output = "I couldn't read that."
  No admin rights are requested. What needs them is reported as "I can't see that without admin rights".

9c. System logs (about 40 lines)
  - Source: the Windows event log, "System" and "Application", errors and critical events of the last
    24 hours (or "since the last restart", "this week" when asked). The Security log is never read.
  - Code groups them by source and event number and counts them. A fixed table turns the known ones into
    plain words. Examples to verify one by one before building:
      unexpected shutdown or power loss      the graphics driver stopped and recovered
      blue screen                            an app crashed or stopped responding (the app's name)
      disk errors                            a Windows service stopped unexpectedly
      hardware error reported by the CPU     Windows ran low on memory
    A short list of known harmless noise is ignored.
  - An unknown event gives source, number and count only. The free text of an event is NOT sent to the
    model and not written anywhere: it can hold user names and file paths.
  - Questions answered by code: "what crashed today", "did the PC shut down badly", "any errors since
    this morning", "how often did the graphics driver crash this week".
  - Also read: Windows' reliability history (app and Windows failures per day), if the timing allows.

9d. The rules that make a finding (a fixed table; thresholds are starting values, tune after a week)
    Finding                                        Rule
    ---------------------------------------------  ------------------------------------------------------
    One app is eating the processor                one app above 50 percent CPU over the sample
    Memory is nearly full                          above 90 percent used; names the largest app
    A disk is nearly full                          below 10 percent or 10 GB free
    A disk is the bottleneck                       busy above 90 percent; names the app writing most
    The GPU is busy / its memory is full           above 90 percent; names the app (JARVIS's own model
                                                   is named as such)
    Running hot / being slowed down by heat        temperature near the limit, or throttling reported
    On battery in a power-saving mode              explains slowness on battery
    The Wi-Fi signal is weak / the router is slow  signal below 40 percent; router ping above 100 ms
    Internet down but Wi-Fi up                     router answers, public address does not
    A device has a problem                         Windows reports a faulty device
    Something crashed                              from 9c, with the count
    A restart is pending / up for many days        Windows flag; uptime above 14 days
    JARVIS is on the CPU                           from Phase 2
  Each finding is one line of numbers and names. "Why is the fan loud" = heat + top CPU app + top GPU app.

9e. What he may do about it
  - Nothing by himself. He names the cause and offers the action that already exists:
    "Chrome is using 71 percent. Want me to close it?" -> the existing close action with its yes.
  - A fixed list of Windows' own processes can never be closed, whatever is said.
  - No forced "end task", no service stop, no driver or update action. These stay with the user.
  - DECIDED by the user on 2026-10-13: he may warn without being asked, but ONLY with the pop-up line on
    the HUD, never aloud. So: a slow watch, one snapshot a minute (about 15 more lines).
      - It uses the same rules as 9d. A finding must hold for 3 checks in a row before it is shown, and
        the same finding is not shown again for 30 minutes.
      - It shows one line through the existing pop-up path (the one a muted alarm uses). No sound, no
        chime, no listening, no model call.
      - It does not run on HUD tier 3 or higher, on battery, or while a turn is busy.
      - Measure its cost first (CPU percent and milliseconds per check, per CLAUDE.md section 4). If one
        check costs more than about 1 percent of a core averaged over the minute, lengthen the interval.
      - Setting JarvisWatch = 1 (0 turns it off).

9f. Privacy
  - Process names and counts go to the LOCAL model only. Window titles are never read (they hold document
    and site names). Nothing from a snapshot is written to JARVIS's log except timings and counts.
  - He can speak app names aloud (the user agreed on 2026-10-13). In STEALTH or muted he shows the line on the HUD only, as today.

Tests (fakes: a fake snapshot, a fake script result, a fake event list)
  - Each trouble phrase routes to diagnosis; "what's using my CPU" is answered without the fake model.
  - A fake snapshot with one app at 80 percent gives exactly that finding, ranked first.
  - 31 fake processes of one app are added up under one name.
  - A clean fake snapshot gives the fixed "nothing wrong" line and the fake model is never called.
  - A fake event with free text: the text reaches neither the prompt nor the log.
  - A script that fails or prints rubbish gives "I couldn't read that", never a guess.
  - The watch: a finding seen once shows nothing; seen 3 times shows one pop-up and plays no sound;
    it is not repeated inside 30 fake minutes; nothing runs on tier 3 or on battery.
  - "close it" after a finding asks for a yes; a Windows system process is refused.
  - The model prompt holds findings only, and holds none of them for an unrelated question.

Your live test after Phase 9
  1. Start something heavy (a game or a video export). Ask "why is my PC slow?" He names it.
  2. "What's using my memory?" Compare with Task Manager's Memory column.
  3. "What's using the graphics card?" Compare with Task Manager's GPU column.
  4. "Why is the fan loud?" He gives the temperature and the app behind it.
  5. "Did anything crash today?" Compare with Event Viewer, Windows Logs.
  6. Switch the router off for a moment. "Is my internet working?"
  7. With nothing wrong: "Is anything wrong?" He says no and gives the numbers.
  8. Leave something heavy running for five minutes without asking. A pop-up line appears, silently.
  9. Time from the end of your question to his first word. Target: under 4 seconds once on the GPU.

Unverified until built and run: the exact counter names on this machine, per-process GPU numbers on a
hybrid laptop, which events appear without admin rights, and the script's run time.
Risk: a wrong rule blames the wrong app. He only names and offers; closing still needs your yes.
Risk: a game can show high CPU and be fine. The rules say "is using", never "is broken".
Rollback: revert the commit. Nothing is installed, stored or changed on the system by this phase.

PHASE 10B  FIXES FROM THE 2026-10-17/20 LIVE TEST + VERIFIED GEMINI USABILITY ITEMS   (added 2026-10-20; PLAN ONLY, nothing built)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Sources: "User complaints - from live test.md" (the user's live test with the full jarvis.log) and
  "jarvis_user_improvment.md" (Gemini usability list). Every claim below was checked in the code on 2026-10-20 (file:line);
  the Gemini items kept are only the ones that held up. Placed before Phase 11 on the user's word.
  The user's decisions (2026-10-20):
    - Wake word: train a SYNTHETIC model (S2-4 pulled forward). The user's own voice checks stay only as an extra
      filter and never wake him alone.
    - Gemini: verified small fixes only (U1-U5). The one-time setup wizard stays in Stage 2.
    - GPU: measure first, then the smallest fix the numbers support (CLAUDE.md section 4).
  Each item still gets goal / scope / non-goals / acceptance / rollback (CLAUDE.md section 3) and the user's word before
  it starts. Order: trust first (W), then flow (T), then GPU (G), then the small fixes (U, X).

  W  WAKE WORD
  W1 A BARE "JARVIS" WAKES HIM (complaint 7)
     Evidence: log 23:50:36 "wake word heard (0.04, HJ)" then "you: 1 words". HJ = the trained "hey" check opens a 5 s
     window and the trained "jarvis" check then needs only 0.35 (jarvis.py:1418-1421); the base model's score (0.04) is
     not asked. HV = base score >= 0.3 AND the trained hey_jarvis check >= 0.5 (jarvis.py:1416-1417). W0 = base score alone.
     Fix: remove the HJ branch and the hey window; drop JarvisWakeEasy / JarvisWakeHard and their Control Center row
     (control.lua:612-614). HV and W0 stay, so "Hey Jarvis" / "Hey Clover" still wake him; a lone name does not.
     Stop-gap today, no code: Control Center HEY...JARVIS -> OFF (sets JarvisWakeEasy=0).
     Test (fakes): base 0.04 + jarvis check 0.9 -> no wake; base 0.8 -> wakes; HV unchanged.
     Live: say only "Jarvis" 10 times (no wake), "Hey Jarvis" 10 times (wake).
  W2 SYNTHETIC "HEY CLOVER" MODEL (complaint 6; = S2-4, moved here)
     The user asked: would recordings from an online generator beat the training, and is it needed for "Hey Clover" anyway?
     Answer: yes to both. openWakeWord's shipped models were trained this way, on thousands of generated voices, so the
     model works for any voice and room and does not depend on one muted or interrupted session. "Hey Clover" needs it
     anyway, because the shipped model only knows "Hey Jarvis". The user's own recorded checks (.pkl) remain useful to
     reject other people and TV, but only as a filter on top of the base score (HV), never alone.
     Steps: train once OFF this PC (Colab or a throw-away folder; the official notebook may need a patched version, see
     S2-4) -> Jarvis\wake\hey_clover.onnx -> JarvisWakeModel=hey_clover.onnx. The loader and the fallback to Hey Jarvis
     exist (jarvis.py:1291-1306, 1453-1464; commits 0bbb1cc, 7d6894b). The Jarvis\wake\ folder does not exist yet, and a
     change of JarvisWakeModel needs a service restart (the model loads once, jarvis.py:1494). Only .onnx works.
     Retrain the voice checks after switching (they are not tied to a model).
     Pass rule unchanged: 9 of 10 (B1) and 0 false wakes (B2) before it replaces Hey Jarvis. Never load a model file from
     someone else. Needs the user's word and a decision on where it is trained.
  W3 TRAINING THAT CANNOT BE MISSED OR INTERRUPTED (complaints 1, 3, 4)
     - Volume off (complaint 1): the prompts are spoken (jarvis.py:1349, 1357) and the ding has no text. When muted the
       prompt only reaches the pop-up line, which is cleared at the end of speaking (live("")). Fix: at the start, warn on
       the HUD ("Volume is off - read the steps here"); keep a step line with a counter ("Say 'Hey Clover' - 3 of 6")
       until the next step; the ding gets a text/flash cue.
     - Interrupted (complaint 4): while the lock is held and he is speaking, the wake loop takes the mic for barge-in, so a
       stop word, "Hey Jarvis" or (BargeIn=voice) any speech cancels training (jarvis.py:1488, 1535-1537, 1559-1587).
       Fix: no barge-in and no stop words while training holds the lock. Only a click, the End key or QUIT cancels it.
       A cancel during the processing step does nothing today (flag checked only at jarvis.py:1354 and 1360); check it
       there too.
     - A failed openwakeword import dies silently (jarvis.py:1339-1342 sit before the try): show it on the HUD.
     - Chat while muted (complaint 2) is T5.
     Test (fakes): training with barge-in conditions -> not cancelled; muted -> step text with counter is shown.
  T  TURN FLOW
  T1 "NEVER MIND" SHOULD SAY NOTHING (complaint 5)
     Evidence: routing.py:394-395 returns ("say", ..., "Okay, never mind.") when "never mind" appears anywhere. The silent
     ENDERS path already exists for a whole-reply "never mind" (jarvis.py:673-676, 1005-1008). Fix: the same silence, no
     reply, no follow-up. Known side case, kept as is: "never mind the timer, set an alarm" drops the alarm
     (test_jarvis.py:3070). Other cancel replies ("Okay, no timer.", "Cancelled.") stay: they confirm something was undone.
     Test: "set a timer, never mind" -> no spoken reply, no timer, no listening afterwards.
  T2 FOLLOW-UP ONLY WHERE IT BELONGS (complaint 8; audit BUG-P8-04)
     Evidence: Slice D said "chat and info only; after a command a 5 s undo window"; S2-10 replaced it with one window after
     EVERY reply (jarvis.py:967 fixed(), :1209 chat). The log shows the memory save and the recall answer both opening
     "listening". Wanted (the user): nothing but direct commands skips the follow-up, and even commands listen for an undo
     for a couple of seconds. Recalling memory goes back to a normal answer: it keeps the follow-up only if the
     chat/info rules say so ("After recalling memory it can directly skip follow-up mode").
     Fix: fixed() leaves mode "undo" for command kinds (act, remind/timer, memory save, multi, level) filtered by
     way["kind"]; exchange() gets its own short wait (about 2 s) because follow_wait() cannot go below 3 s (mic.py:126) and
     follow_name_wait() stretches to 10 s (jarvis.py:894-895); in that window only route()[0]=="undo" counts. Chat-like
     fixed replies ("I heard...", diag, "new topic") are NOT commands: filter on kind, not on fixed().
     Memory recall: the topic recall (jarvis.py:1162) feeds a chat answer; save the result and skip the ear at :1209.
     Test: after each kind, the mode is as above; speech that is not "undo" in the undo window is dropped.
  T3 LISTEN ONE SECOND AFTER A STOP (complaint 9)
     Evidence: after a stop turn() returns (jarvis.py:839, cancel set) and nothing listens. Fix: a stop by stop word or End
     key while he speaks -> mode "ear" with a 1 s wait and no 0.3 s sleep (jarvis.py:883); speech counts as a follow-up.
     A click-cancel or QUIT does not reopen listening. Test: stop word, then "set a timer" within 1 s -> taken.
  T4 SLOWER ANSWERS (complaints 10, 13; measure first)
     Evidence (jarvis.log): follow-ups reply in 0.3-0.4 s; every WOKEN chat turn needs 3.6 s to the first sentence
     (10-17 23:36:52 4.28 s, 23:44:17 3.62 s ...), and on 10-20 6.8-9.3 s (llama3.2:3b) with 20.3 s once
     (10:38:32, llama3.1:8b), with whisper medium.en loaded. "Stop" took long: reply done 35.8 s.
     Suspects, each to be measured before any fix:
       a. warm(model) runs on every new turn (jarvis.py:885) with no num_ctx (llm.py:95); chat uses 2048 (llm.py:147). If
          Ollama's default differs, the model reloads on every woken turn. Matches "only woken turns are slow". Check
          `ollama ps` (context column) and the Ollama server log.
       b. The busy lock is held while ollama_on_cpu() runs (HTTP, up to 2 s, llm.py:104) and chat_to_gpu() may reload the
          model (jarvis.py:1181, llm.py:116-131). The HUD already shows idle, so a click then is silently dropped (:848).
       c. HUD tier >= 1 swaps to JarvisModelFast (llm.py:66-82): a reload; medium.en shares the VRAM.
       d. No stable prompt prefix: "Now: HH:MM" and per-turn blocks sit first in the system message (prompt.py:62), so the
          whole history (up to 40 messages / 1650 tokens, memory.py:58, jarvis.py:624) is processed again every turn.
       e. MiniLM embedding work before the model call (history, recall, facts; meaning.py): compare the log lines
          "end of speech -> reply start" (this) with "reply start -> first sentence" (the model).
       f. Abandoned stream after a stop still being read (llm.py:159-162) delays the next request.
     Fix only what the numbers show. Likely smallest: the same options in warm(), the on-CPU check outside the lock, the
     time line moved to the end of the prompt. The user's whisper medium.en choice (10-20) is to be re-measured too.
     Acceptance: woken chat turn, end of speech to first audio, under 2 s on the GPU (numbers in the plan before/after).
  T5 TYPE TO HIM IN THE CHAT POP-UP WHILE MUTED (complaint 2)
     Evidence: the only text box is [mAsk] in the Control Center (Control.ini:46-57, control.lua:595-596, AskJarvis
     control.lua:951-956). Overlay\Jarvis (Jarvis.ini, jarvislog.lua) has scroll, close and right-click only.
     Fix: an InputText measure + hit zone in the pop-up that writes the same "ASK <unix> <text>" line. Typed turns keep
     their rules (untrusted, 500 characters, a typed turn never follows up, no voice "yes": jarvis.py:803-805, 839).
     Optional on the user's word: queue an ASK that arrives while he is busy instead of dropping it (jarvis.py:1710-1711).
     Test: ASK reaches route() unchanged; muted reply shows in the pop-up; too-long line cut.
  T6 "C.L.O.V.E.R" DOES NOT CHANGE (complaint 11)
     Evidence: the label is fixed text at jarvis.lua:371 and jarvislog.lua:70 (also the speaker tag 'CLOVER' at
     jarvislog.lua:86 and tooltips jarvis.lua:376-379). The NAME buttons only write JarvisName (control.lua:738-741) and
     push it live to Center\Sphere (control.lua:462-466); nothing reads it for these labels.
     Fix: build the label from JarvisName (letters with dots, upper case) in both files and push !SetVariable to
     Overlay\Jarvis as well. Lua is unverified until a live run (W5 of the bug list).
  G  GPU AT ABOUT 80 PERCENT, DROPS WHEN RAINMETER CLOSES (complaint 12)
     Likely Rainmeter drawing, not the JARVIS daemon (0 percent GPU busy idle in the M0 baseline, plan l.2558). Not measured on this PC.
  G1 MEASURE (about 5 minutes, read-only, the user does it; numbers go in the plan)
     1. QUIT JARVIS from the Control Center, leave Rainmeter running: does the load stay near 80 percent?
     2. Task Manager > Details > add GPU and GPU engine columns: which GPU is Rainmeter.exe on (the Radeon or the RTX)?
        nvidia-smi --query-gpu=utilization.gpu --format=csv -l 1 shows whether the RTX is busy at all.
     3. Control Center > PERFORMANCE: FULL / LITE / LOW / STEALTH, about 30 s each.
     4. Unload one skin at a time: Center\Sphere, Center\HUD, Center\Target, Bottom\Audio, Left and Right Category.
     5. Compare the HUD's own GPU number (control.lua:334 NOW row) with Task Manager: UsageMonitor may add up both GPUs

  G2 FIX BY THE NUMBERS (in this order; stop when the target is met)
     1. PerfOverride=0 pins FULL (Settings.inc:76, controller.lua:113-115), so the automatic tiers never act. Set -1.
     2. Sphere updates every 33 ms (210-frame PNG sprite drawn twice + 56-point network with 3 glow passes,
        jarvis.lua:70-145, lib.lua:169-179); HUD, Target, Audio every 50 ms. JarvisFrameRate=15 changes only the animation
        speed, not the redraw rate. Raise the Update/UpdateDivider per tier.
     3. Storage.ini redraws the whole canvas every 100 ms with no tier check (storage.lua:18-54): redraw only on change.
     4. Text glow and AccurateText/DynamicWindowSize on every module (Common.inc:3-4, lib.lua:396-400) cost more on FULL.
     5. TierStealth default is 90 in control.lua:339 but 85 in Settings.inc:80: make them agree.
     Acceptance: GPU percent before/after for each change in the plan; nothing visibly broken; JARVIS functions unchanged.
     Rollback: the settings; git revert.
  U  GEMINI ITEMS THAT HELD UP IN THE CODE (small, independent)
  U1 CONFIDENCE FROM THE WORST SEGMENT: hearing.py:110 takes the minimum avg_logprob of all segments, so one noisy tail
     forces "Did you say: ...?" (jarvis.py:774). Use a word-weighted mean that ignores 1-word trailing segments.
     Measure both sides first (W3 of the bug list: never a threshold by feel). Test: a clear command + a noisy 1-word tail
     -> no question; a really unsure command -> still asks.
  U2 NEAR-MISS RECOVERY COUNTS FILLER: fix_route gives up above 8 words counted BEFORE tidy() (routing.py:283), so
     "Hey Jarvis, could you please turn the volume down" skips the typo recovery. Count after tidy(). Run check_route.py:
     0 routes changed except the intended ones.
  U3 THE SILENCE WAIT: JarvisSilence=2 (Settings.inc:238) is a mandatory 2 s after every command. First measure in the log how
     long the user's mid-sentence pauses are (not by feel); then choose a lower default (about 1.2 s) and keep it a setting.
     The two-tier (short for short commands / longer for unfinished clauses) and the Silero gate stay in Stage 2.
  U4 KOKORO PATH: Setup-Jarvis.ps1 installs Kokoro into Jarvis\voices\, but Settings.inc:234 points to Jarvis\kokoro and
     resolve_voice() searches only dirname(voice) and Jarvis\kokoro (tts.py:193): a fresh install falls back to the
     Windows voice. Add Jarvis\voices to the search. Test: a fake file in voices\ is found. (This PC already works.)
  U5 THE MISSED-WORDS ANSWER: "Sorry, I didn't catch that." (jarvis.py:922) says nothing about why. First miss: "Pardon,
     <callme>?"; a near-silent peak: "I barely heard anything - check the microphone."; a second miss in a row: open
     the type box (T5). Wording goes through the Phase 10 tone rules (the user's hand review).
  X  FOUND WHILE READING (verify first)
  X1 prompt.py:137 builds PERSONA from RES + "@Resources" + ..., but RES already is the @Resources folder (the other files use
     join(RES, "Jarvis", ...)), so persona.txt is probably never read and the built-in text is used. The tests replace
     PERSONA and cannot see it. First add a log line "persona: loaded <path> / not found"; fix only if confirmed live.
  PARKED TO STAGE 2 (Gemini): unified one-time setup wizard; Silero-VAD silence gate; clause-aware first TTS chunk (= 6g);
     topic-continuity check before ROOM_ASKS; push-to-talk hotkey stays rejected.
  Rollback for the phase: git revert the item's commit; settings return to their old values; Jarvis\wake\ files can be deleted.

FIX ROUND 3   FROM THE THIRD LIVE TEST (added 2026-10-14)
  Round 1, plain code (built, waiting for the user's live test):
    - A follow-up without the wake word after command replies too, and once after "Sorry, I didn't catch
      that." Not after "Okay, never mind.", "Okay, no X." or "Cancelled.". Still at most 3, and JarvisFollowUp=0
      turns it off. Command replies now go into the chat history, so he knows an alarm was cancelled.
    - "Undo that" after a cancel puts the items back if they have not rung yet.
    - Clock words: o'clock, in the morning / evening, tonight, noon, midnight, number words ("seven thirty"),
      "1900 hours" and a leading 0 mean the 24-hour clock, and "tomorrow" / "today".
    - "No, AM" / "make it PM" just after an alarm is set moves it 12 hours.
    - Several alarms at once ("7 and 8 am"), and two safe commands in one sentence ("an alarm for 7 and a
      timer for 10 minutes"). A command that needs a confirmation is never split out.
    Limits: in a two-command sentence, undo reverses only the last one. An undo after "no, AM" removes the
    alarm instead of moving it back.
  Round 2 (built 2026-10-14, waiting for the live test): one schedule, time grounding, and an AI fallback.
    Measured first: llama3.1:8b as the first reader of every request got 75 of 97 phrases right and turned 14
    questions into actions, so the AI is only a checked fallback, never the first reader.
    - One schedule: "what reminders / alarms do I have" lists alarms and reminders together; "cancel the
      reminder" / "cancel all reminders" search both; a clock time with no am / pm matches either 7. Timers
      stay apart. Undo puts a cancel back. Storage is unchanged.
    - Time grounding: Now has a 12-hour form; the Pending line gives each item's type and time (never its
      label, at most 5); command replies in the chat history carry a day and time stamp. The system prompt no
      longer says "you cannot set alarms"; it says the system does, and the chat never claims to.
    - AI fallback (interpret): only for a near-miss, or a chat line with an alarm / timer / reminder / volume
      / media word that is not a question or about the past. The model names an intent from a fixed list and
      copies the words; code checks the words were said, builds a fixed sentence and route() must read it as
      that command. Lock, sleep, restart, shutdown, open, close, memory and to-do are never on its list. Any
      error, timeout (8 s) or doubt: the answer is exactly as before. JarvisAIRouter=0 turns it off.
    He says "One moment." as the AI call starts; his answer waits for it to finish.
    Limits: real accuracy and the call's time on this PC are unmeasured.
  Round 3: "Hey Jarvis" needing two tries. First log near-miss scores (numbers only), then fix. Not built yet.
  Round 4: common assistant complaints (built 2026-10-14, waiting for the live test). Found with ~90 phrases
    and no mic. Fixed:
    - Times: "remind me to call mom tomorrow at 9" was set for TODAY with "tomorrow" in the label (now
      tomorrow, label "to call mom"). Also new: "tomorrow at 9 to ...", "on Friday at 9", "Monday at 7", "next
      Friday at 9 am" (a weekday is that day, at most 7 days ahead; today's weekday with its time gone is next
      week), "half past six", "quarter to 8", "ten past 7", "7 tomorrow morning", "6 tomorrow night",
      "a minute and a half".
    - "Turn off my alarm" with several alarms asks "You have 2 alarms: 7:00 AM and 8:00 AM. Which one?" and
      listens once ("the 7", "both", or anything else = "Okay, I left them."). "My alarms" / "all" / "both"
      means every one, no question.
    - The same alarm or reminder twice (within a minute) is kept once: "You already have an alarm for 7:00 AM."
    - "What time is my alarm set for" lists them.
    - Change one: "move / change my alarm to 8", "make the timer 10 minutes", "add 5 minutes to the timer".
      Asks which when several match; undo puts it back.
    - "Snooze (for N)" up to 5 minutes after the ringing stopped. Otherwise "Nothing is ringing right now."
      "Snoozed" / "moved" are added to the false-claim filter.
    - In a follow-up only, a whole reply of thanks / that's all / no thanks / nope / goodbye / I'm good /
      nothing / stop ends it silently. On a turn you started, "thanks" still gets a reply.
    - Whisper's phantom lines ("Thanks for watching!", "Subtitles by ...", a lone "you") are silence.
      Log: "transcript: dropped (phantom)", no words.
    - "Turn it up by 20" / "lower the volume by 5 percent".
    Known limits:
    - Muted Windows: the alarm is a pop-up only (the user chose to keep this).
    - No barge-in (talking over him); a click stops him. (Barge-in and the End key came in Phase 4.)
      No "mute for 10 minutes". No dates past 7 days.
    - The log keeps "you: <what you said>". The user decided to keep it (2026-10-14): no change.
    - A real "Thank you." heard while an alarm rings still stops it (STOP_WORDS), as before.
    - Moving a weekly repeating reminder to another weekday keeps its old weekday.
    - "Remind me to buy milk for Monday at 9" leaves "for" at the end of the label.
    Unverified until the live test: real Whisper phantom lines from this mic, and how "Which one?" feels.
