========================================================================================================
STAGE 2   THE LARGER IDEAS (added 2026-10-14 from the user's feature list; AFTER Phases 7-10 are done)
========================================================================================================
  MODULES (REFACTOR R-END, 2026-10-15): see "WHERE EACH LATER ITEM GOES" in the REFACTOR block.
Why a separate stage: each item here is big, crosses several files or systems, or brings in a new
service. None starts until Phases 7-10 are built and live-tested. Each item gets its own plan (goal,
scope, non-goals, acceptance, validation, risks, rollback; CLAUDE.md section 3) and the user's approval.
The small parts of the same list are already in: Fix Round 5 (wake on noise), 7e (Control Center
sections + memory row), 8c (quiet in class), 8d (typing), 8e (think hard), Phase 10 (name, smoother voice).

Rules that hold for all of Stage 2
  R1. Model output never authorizes an OS action by itself (CLAUDE.md section 5). A model may PROPOSE;
      fixed code checks the proposal against a fixed list, and anything consequential asks for a yes
      (the shared YES set).
  R2. Low RAM / CPU: nothing new runs in the background unless measured first. Every new part is idle at
      0 percent CPU and loads its model only when used. One Ollama model on the graphics card at a time.
      Measure before and after (CPU, RAM, VRAM, the 6j timing lines), as CLAUDE.md section 4 asks.
  R3. Secrets never enter git and are never printed or logged (see S2-5).
  R4. Each item has a Control Center switch, default OFF, and turning it off returns today's behaviour.

Suggested order (cheapest and most useful first): S2-1, S2-5, S2-7, S2-6, S2-4, S2-2, S2-3.
Claude Code (S2-2) and vision (S2-3) come last, as the user asked.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S2-1  ONE CLEAR PIPELINE (hear -> sort -> the right handler)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  The user's design:
    Whisper hears -> an interpreter sorts what was said into a few groups plus how hard it is ->
      chat           -> the chat model; it may offer to turn the chat into a command / task, after a yes
      very complex   -> a bigger model ("think really hard about this"; 8e is the first, small slice)
      command / task -> a simple handler that runs a fixed Python action ("volume up by 20")
      complex task   -> gathers the context it needs and goes to Claude Code (S2-2), saying
                        "Executing task, one moment sir."
  What exists today and is kept: route() (fixed rules, instant), fix_route() (near-miss), interpret()
  (the AI fallback, Fix Round 3 Round 2), ask_ai(), pick_model(tier), meaning() + the MiniLM embedder.
  The lesson already measured here: llama3.1:8b as FIRST reader got 75 of 97 right and turned 14
  questions into actions. So the model must not be the first reader.
  How others do it:
    - Home Assistant Assist: fixed local sentences first, the LLM only for what they miss (PART H).
    - semantic-router (aurelio-labs): each route is a handful of example sentences, embedded once; a new
      sentence is embedded and the nearest route wins, with no LLM call. Used exactly for "skip the slow
      model for the decision".
    - "When to Reason" (vLLM Semantic Router paper): send only the questions that need reasoning to the
      reasoning model, decided by an embedding classifier.
  Design for JARVIS:
    1. route() stays first: a clear command never waits for anything (0 ms, as today).
    2. NEW sorter for what route() does not take: the MiniLM embedder JARVIS already loads (meaning(),
       ~12 KB per cached sentence) compares the sentence to 10-20 example sentences per group:
       chat | deep question | command-like | task-for-Claude. This is semantic-router's method WITHOUT the
       package (about 40 lines, reusing vec() / meaning()). Cost: one embedding, a few ms on CPU, no model
       load. The examples live in a fixed table in jarvis.py, like BLOCK_EXAMPLES.
    3. command-like -> interpret() as today (the model names an intent from the fixed list, code checks).
    4. deep -> the JarvisModelDeep tier (8e). Only when the sorter is sure OR the user says "think hard".
    5. task-for-Claude -> S2-2, only if JarvisClaude=1, always with a yes first.
    6. chat -> ask_ai as today. If the chat reply decides it should act, it can only OFFER: "Want me to
       set that as a reminder?" -> a yes -> the sentence goes back through route(). The chat never acts.
    7. When unsure (two groups close): chat. Never an action by default.
  Measure before building: the same 97-phrase list (Fix Round 3) through the sorter: how many land in the
  right group, and how many questions go to command-like (target: 0 to an action without a yes).
  RAM / CPU: no new model; the embedder is already resident. The deep model is loaded only per question.
  Risk: wrong group -> chat answers a command. Acceptable: it is the safe direction.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S2-2  CLAUDE CODE FOR COMPLEX TASKS (and changes to his own code)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  The user's design: JARVIS recognises a complex task ("change your code so you can ...", "fix that error
  you have", "look at what I'm doing and ..."), gathers the needed context and hands it to Claude Code.
  A Control Center switch turns it on / off (JarvisClaude, default 0, in JARVIS SETTINGS).
  How others do it:
    - Claude Code headless mode: `claude -p "<task>"` runs without the screen UI and prints the result;
      --output-format json gives a result object (fields differ between sources: read the real output);
      --allowedTools limits which tools it may use, and Bash can be limited to one command pattern
      (e.g. Bash(py -3 test_jarvis.py)); --permission-mode controls edits; a session id can be resumed.
      VERIFY every flag with Context7 / `claude --help` on this machine before building.
    - Open Interpreter: asks for confirmation before running any generated code; its "safe mode" is
      described by its own docs as giving no guarantees. Lesson: the yes before running is the real guard.
  Design for JARVIS:
    1. The sorter (S2-1) or a fixed phrase ("ask Claude to ...", "change your code so ...") picks a task.
    2. Code builds a TASK BRIEF from a fixed template: the user's words, the route / error lines he
       already holds in memory (fixed problem texts, not the log file), and which files are in scope.
       Nothing private is added (no memory facts, no calendar, no mail) unless the user says so.
    3. He shows the brief on the HUD and says "Send this to Claude Code?" -> yes (shared YES set).
       Then: "Executing task, one moment sir."
    4. Code runs `claude -p` as a subprocess with a FIXED argument list (no shell, no string built from
       speech), a fixed working folder (the repo, never the live skin), a tight --allowedTools list, a
       time limit, and the exit code checked (CLAUDE.md section 5).
    5. Changes to his own code: Claude Code works on a NEW git branch (or worktree) in the repo, runs
       test_jarvis.py, and stops. JARVIS says "Done. 2 files changed, tests OK. The diff is on screen."
       It is NEVER copied to live, committed or pushed by JARVIS; the user reviews and does those, as now.
    6. Result: a short summary is spoken; the full text is shown on the HUD / a file the user opens.
  Never allowed (fixed list, code-checked): anything outside the repo folder, git push, deleting files,
  installing packages, editing Settings.inc or Data\, network calls other than Claude's own.
  RAM / CPU: Claude Code runs in the cloud; locally only the CLI process while a task runs (measure it).
  Nothing runs when idle. Cost: it uses the user's Claude plan / API usage; show the task's cost from the
  JSON result if available.
  Risks: prompt injection from file contents into Claude Code (it only reaches the repo, and every change
  is reviewed); a long task holding the turn (run it in the background, he stays usable, a pop-up when
  done); cost. Acceptance: a test task ("add a comment to X") on a branch, tests run, nothing else
  touched, the switch off = the phrase gets "Claude Code is off."

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S2-3  "LOOK AT WHAT I'M DOING" (computer vision)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  The user's design: he can see the screen to help with a task.
  How others do it: a hobby assistant switches Ollama from a fast text model to a vision model
  (llama3.2-vision) only when asked to look, with a screenshot taken at that moment. Open Interpreter
  runs fully local with Ollama. (Single sources; treat as ideas, not proof.)
  Design for JARVIS:
    - ON REQUEST ONLY: one screenshot when the user says "look at this / what I'm doing". Never a
      continuous watch (that is the Windows Recall problem: a constant record of private screens).
    - Two routes, the user picks per setting (JarvisVision = off | local | claude, default off):
        local:  a vision model in Ollama (e.g. llama3.2-vision 11B, or a smaller one). Heavy on VRAM;
                loaded only for that question, unloaded after (keep_alive 0). Measure load time first.
        claude: the screenshot goes with the S2-2 task to Claude. It LEAVES the PC: he asks every time
                ("This sends your screen to Claude. Go ahead?").
    - The screenshot is kept in memory only and never saved, unless the user asks. Not logged.
    - This changes the Phase 9f rule ("window titles are never read") for this one, asked-for moment;
      it does not change it anywhere else.
    - What he may DO after seeing: only offer, as everywhere (R1). Seeing is not a reason to act.
  Risks: private content on screen (asked each time, nothing stored); a wrong reading (he says what he
  sees, the user decides).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S2-4  A NEW NAME AS THE WAKE WORD
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  MOVED 2026-10-20 (the user) to PHASE 10B, item W2: the synthetic model is trained there. The text below stays as its spec.
  Phase 10 adds the name he uses for himself (JarvisName). Waking on a new name needs a new model:
  openWakeWord's own models were trained from 100 percent synthetic (text-to-speech) speech; a new word
  is trained the same way on top of its shared feature part (its notebook: "under an hour"). A 2026
  community report says the official Colab notebook no longer runs on the default runtime and offers a
  patched one (75-90 min on Colab Pro); local pipelines need many pinned packages and ~45 GB disk.
  Design: train once, OFF this PC (Colab or a throw-away folder), produce one .onnx file; JARVIS loads it
  with the same Model(...) call (wakeword_models=[path]). Nothing is installed in the venv for it. The
  Fix Round 5 checks (trained "Hey" / name checks) are retrained with the user's voice. "Hey Jarvis"
  stays as a fallback setting until the new word reaches 9 of 10 (B1) with 0 false wakes (B2).
  RAM / CPU: one more small onnx model only if both words are on (about the same cost as today's).
  Risk: a home-made model is weaker than the shipped one; it must pass B1 / B2 before replacing it.
  A model file from someone else is never loaded (same rule as the .pkl files).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S2-5  ONLINE BASICS: WEATHER, NEWS (and where keys live)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  The user asked for easy, fixed API keys for basic online info. Cheapest first: NO key at all.
    - Weather: Open-Meteo needs no key and no account (free for non-commercial use; data from national
      weather services). The location: the HUD's own location.ps1 result if it has one, else a fixed
      town in Settings.
    - News: the RSS feeds of a few chosen outlets need no key. Headlines only (title + source), at most 5.
    - Anything that DOES need a key: stored the way the HUD already stores the calendar links ("Stored
      encrypted for your Windows account", ShipCore.cs) or in Data\ (outside git, gitignored). Never in
      Settings.inc, never in the repo, never printed, logged or spoken. The Control Center shows only
      "key set" / "no key".
  Design: fetched ON DEMAND when a question needs it (a new BLOCKS entry "weather" / "news", like the
  calendar block), cached 30 minutes, 5 s timeout, the answer says "I couldn't reach the weather service"
  on any failure (truth rule). The fetched text is untrusted: only numbers and headline text are used;
  never instructions.
  RAM / CPU: one HTTPS request per question at most every 30 min; nothing in the background.
  Risk: an outlet changes its feed (the answer says it couldn't read it).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S2-6  VOICE BLENDING (the larger version)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Phase 10 / 6g does the cheap part: measure the gaps, join very short sentences, short fades.
  Stage 2 only if that is not enough: speak the first sentence while the model is still writing the next
  (streaming), and cross-fade the joins (20-40 ms) so sentences flow. Kokoro is already the voice; the
  change is in speak_all()'s queue, not a new engine. Measure first: time to first word and the gap
  between sentences from the 6j timing lines, before and after.
  RAM / CPU: the same voice model; a few ms of mixing per join.
  Risk: barge-in (Phase 4) must still stop him at once while streaming; tested first.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S2-7  MEMORY EDITOR IN THE CONTROL CENTER
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  7e gives the JARVIS MEMORY section a count and OPEN (Notepad). Stage 2: each fact as its own row with
  EDIT and DELETE, plus the topic memory (jarvis_topics.json) with DELETE per topic. Lua reads the files
  directly (Rainmeter's Lua can read files, as control.lua does); writes go through one command line in
  jarvis_cmd.txt (FORGETFACT <n>) so the daemon is the only writer (no two writers on one file). Undo
  works the same as a spoken "forget".
  Risk: Lua row count; a long list needs paging (10 per page).

  S2-8 .. S2-13 come from the user's 2026-10-14 live log + VS Code diagnostics. All six were
  implemented 2026-10-15 (uncommitted; each has a STATUS line below).
  The user's rule for them: fix the pipeline and strengthen the infrastructure, do not patch. S2-8 and
  S2-9 go first; the others lean on them.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S2-8  TRUTH LAYER (he must not be ABLE to claim a change that did not happen)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Seen: he said he changed brightness (a Phase 7 setting that does not exist yet). "Can you tell me if I
  have any alarms for tomorrow?" went to chat, which called a calendar event (MATH 251 - Exam II) an
  alarm, invented "Class Due times", then two turns later said "I don't have any record of setting an
  alarm for 9:00 AM". "Have you been?" -> an invented meeting with "the Emperor" (tone part: Phase 10).
  Cause: CLAIM (jarvis.py ~l.1896) only knows alarm/timer/reminder/volume words and a short "I've set..."
  list, so no_claims() (~l.1958) lets "I've dimmed the screen" through. The near-miss catch (~l.2170)
  only fires when the sentence STARTS with set/turn/change...; "can you dim the screen" goes to the model.
  Calendar events and user alarms sit in the chat context without a label telling them apart.
  Fix (infrastructure, not one more regex word):
    - One capability table in code: what exists (alarm, timer, reminder, volume, mute, lock, media, apps,
      sleep, restart...) and what does not yet (brightness, Bluetooth, mic, Wi-Fi, display...). Phase 7's
      7-0 action table IS this table; build it once and both use it.
    - A request that names a setting with no capability gets a fixed reply by code ("I can't do that
      yet.") before the model is called. Code decides; the model never sees it.
    - Chat may never state a change: the claim filter covers every verb and every setting in the table.
      A state answer ("is it muted?", "what alarms do I have?") comes only from a read-back by code.
    - Alarm/timer/reminder QUESTIONS always take the rule lane (one answer, every phrasing). Calendar
      events reach the chat prompt labelled as calendar, never as alarms.
  Reuse: route(), no_claims(), did_add()/DID, run_action(), remind_reply().
  Risk: over-blocking a real question ("why is my screen dim?"): questions stay chat, only requests are
  stopped; route check + test_jarvis cases for both.
  STATUS 2026-10-15: done. CAPABILITY check in route(): UNBUILT topics (brightness, Bluetooth, Wi-Fi,
  night light, dark mode, wallpaper, mic, hibernate, sign out...) + a request verb -> CANT "I can't do
  that yet." by code, logged "capability: not built yet - said so"; questions ("why is my screen dim?")
  stay chat. CLAIM covers many more I-verbs, "I'll <verb>", "<setting> is now on/off/..." and "Done".
  Alarm questions in every tested phrasing go to the rule list; calendar lines are labelled "(calendar
  events, not alarms or reminders)". Limit: UNBUILT is a regex list, not yet the 7-0 action table.
  2nd pass 2026-10-15: UNBUILT is now a named table, UNBUILT_CAPS (brightness, bluetooth, wifi, display,
  microphone, account, power plan, keyboard light, notifications); the log names the row: "capability:
  brightness not built yet - said so". Built = ACTIONS + the reminder / volume / media lanes. Phase 7-0
  folds both into one action table (still needs the user's layout approval).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S2-9  OUTCOME LOG (every claim checkable from the log)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Seen: rule-lane replies have no "jarvis:" line; actions log only the action name; "never mind, undo"
  took 11.8 s and two spoken lines with no outcome line; a "remind cancel" left no "reminder cancelled"
  line (it most likely took the "Which one?" / "Okay, I left them" path, ~l.2985-2996, which logs
  nothing); "interpreter: fallback (ValueError)" hides the reason interpret() raised.
  Fix: every route ends in one outcome line: lane, action, target CLASS (never your words), result read
  back (done / left / refused + why). Log the interpret() refusal reason. A wake check that learned
  nothing (training "Hey peaks 0.00 (26 examples)") is dropped from "your checks", with a log line.
  Also unexplained, log enough to find it: speech events every ~29 s with no route (23:25:10, 25:39,
  26:08, 26:37, 27:04).
  Risk: log size; one line per turn, no words of yours.
  STATUS 2026-10-15: done in part. interpreter fallback logs its reason ("not what was said", "volume not
  said", "no preset", "incomplete", else the exception type); reminder ops log "N match - asked which",
  "no clear pick - left them", "none matched"; the ear logs "not for me - stayed quiet" / "unsure -
  asked"; capability refusals log. NOT done: the "Hey peaks 0.00" check is kept (a bare "Hey" scoring 0
  is expected); the ~29 s speech events need the user's next log to trace.
  2nd pass 2026-10-15: every turn that heard speech now ends its route line with "-> <result>": done /
  failed / left - no yes / refused - not built / refused - unclear command / answered / said / error /
  stopped. Turns that logged no route before now do: "route: false_wake via rule ... -> nothing said",
  "route: silence ... -> asked again / nothing said", "route: ear ... -> not for me - quiet / asked if
  for me". The ~29 s events should now show up as one of these (live wake sensitivity 0.3 points at
  false wakes). "Hey peaks 0.00": the word styles ("Hey", bare "Jarvis") learn from word samples and
  never use that score, so the line now reads "train: Hey from 26 word samples"; the check stays.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S2-10  ONE EAR WINDOW + "ARE YOU TALKING TO ME, SIR?"
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Seen: follow-ups end "randomly". Cause: in exchange()'s fixed() (~l.3900) the follow-up depends on the
  lane: command replies only re-listen for undo (and drop anything else: "follow-up: ended (not an
  undo)"); courtesy and enders return without it. Undo also asks "Do you want me to undo that?" first.
  Fix: one window after EVERY reply, every lane: listen 3 s with no wake word.
    - Nothing heard: back to standby.
    - Heard: deterministic code scores whether it was for him (command shape, his name, a question, you/
      your, length, sense). Clearly for him: run it as a normal turn. Unsure: ask, flexible wording
      ("Are you talking to me, sir?"). Not for him or no sense: stay quiet.
    - The model may help classify; it never authorises anything (CLAUDE.md section 5).
    - Undo is just one thing the window accepts; a clear undo target runs without the extra yes.
    - Stop words keep ending everything (they work today).
  Reuse: listen(wait=3) / follow_wait(), room_talk(), ROOM_ASK, NO_FOLLOW, ENDERS.
  Risk: TV / people in the room: the "stay quiet" branch is the default when unsure-and-nonsense.
  STATUS 2026-10-15: done. One "ear" mode after every reply (commands, courtesy hello, chat); "Cancelled."
  and NO_FOLLOW still end it. Window = JarvisFollowUpWait (default now 3 s), 8 s after a question or an
  offer. directed() (code only): name / a command route / question words / "?" / you-your -> run it;
  3+ words otherwise -> asks (ROOM_ASKS rotate: "Are you talking to me, sir?"...); shorter -> quiet.
  Undo in the window runs at once, also "Never mind Jarvis, undo". The old undo-ask step is gone.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S2-11  SLEEP, RESTART AND OTHER SYSTEM COMMANDS BLOCKED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Seen: the user reports sleep, restart and others are blocked. Not yet known which path: the voice
  path needs a plain yes within 15 s (~l.1607); act_sleep raises "Windows refused to sleep"; or the
  Control Center SYSTEM buttons. First step: the user says which (voice or button) and pastes the log
  lines. Fix that path; the spoken yes stays (safety).
  Risk: these act on the PC; test restart with "shutdown /a" ready (10 s delay).
  STATUS 2026-10-15: done for the voice path. Short phrasings now route (sleep, sleep mode, put my PC in
  sleep mode, restart, reboot, shut down, power off, lock, lock it); the yes now accepts "Yes, Jarvis" /
  "yes sir" (is_yes). The confirmation stays. Buttons not changed (no report they fail).
  2nd pass 2026-10-15, ROOT CAUSE: this PC has Modern Standby only (powercfg /a: Standby S0 Low Power
  Idle + Hibernate; S1-S3 not available). SetSuspendState fails there (ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED), so both
  voice sleep ("Windows refused to sleep") and the SYSTEM > SLEEP button (ShipCore "sleep") failed.
  Fix in both: if SetSuspendState fails, broadcast SC_MONITORPOWER off (PostMessage HWND_BROADCAST,
  WM_SYSCOMMAND 0xF170, 2), which is how S0 PCs enter standby; only both failing is an error. jarvis.py
  logs "sleep: suspend refused (error N) - screen off for Modern Standby". Restart / shutdown use
  shutdown.exe and were never blocked by this. Sources: learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/Power/
  system-power-states ; community.osr.com/discussion/comment/304430.

S2-12  "CALL ME" NAME ROW
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  JarvisCallMe is read already (courtesy lines, ~l.3933). Getting new keys into live is solved by COPY TO

  InputText to type the name; written to Settings.inc like the other rows.
  Risk: InputText needs its own meter; check it in the Control Center layout first.
  STATUS 2026-10-15: done. CALL ME row before PERSONALITY: CHANGE opens [mInput] (InputText), CLEAR
  empties it. SetCallMe strips control chars and = ; [ ] # " ' % and caps at 30. A hello keeps the ear
  open. Limit: an apostrophe typed in the box breaks the bang (stripped name never has one).
  2nd pass 2026-10-15: [mInput] now hands the text over as """$UserInput$""" into a variable and
  SetCallMe() reads it, so an apostrophe or quote no longer breaks the bang (it is stripped on save).

S2-13  CONTROL CENTER SMOOTH SCROLL + SCROLL BAR
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Seen: too sensitive, jumps. Cause: OnScroll (Scripts/control.lua ~l.793) moves 2 whole rows per notch;
  the list clamps a first ROW (~l.699-703); the bar (~l.761) is drawn but cannot be dragged.
  Fix: scroll in pixels at a fixed rate per notch (eased over a few frames), and a bar you can drag.
  Risk: Rainmeter redraw rate; ease only while moving, idle costs nothing.
  STATUS 2026-10-15: done within Rainmeter's limits. 1 row per notch (was 2); clicking the bar track
  (hit slot H1_47, OnBar) jumps there. NOT possible in Rainmeter: drag, pixel-smooth scrolling.
  2nd pass 2026-10-15: drag added: the track is 16 hit slices (H1_47..H1_62); mouse-down starts a drag,
  hovering a slice while dragging jumps there, mouse-up / leaving the window ends it (1/16 steps).
  Correction: pixel-smooth scrolling IS possible (Rainmeter Container meters clip), but it means
  redrawing the Control Center list, so it waits for the user's approval; rows still move 1 per notch.

  Dev tooling (small, any time): VS Code's warnings are mostly the wrong interpreter (pywebrtc_audio IS
  in %LOCALAPPDATA%\SpaceshipHUD\venv): point the workspace at that venv, mypy ignore_missing_imports.
  Check `used` at jarvis.py ~l.1189 (may be unbound on the GPU branch). The rest are false positives.

S2-14  GUI AUTOMATION: WHEN IT HELPS, WHEN IT HURTS   (the user's idea, 2026-10-15; "consider" only)
  The user asked for this in "phase 2"; PHASE 2 (GPU FIRST) is done, so it sits here in STAGE 2, and
  Phase 7 (7c / 7d settings) checks it per setting. Nothing is built until the user picks.
  What it is: driving an app's window like a person would (Windows UI Automation: find a button or
  toggle by its name / AutomationId, read it, press it) instead of running a command.
  Closing apps (the user's example: don't skip "unsaved changes" warnings):
    - Today act_close already asks politely: taskkill WITHOUT /F sends the close message, the app shows
      its own "Save changes?" box, nothing is skipped, and JARVIS reports "it is still open (it may be
      asking to save)". So GUI automation is NOT needed to protect unsaved work.
    - Where it helps: READ the box. Find the app's open dialog and say "Word is asking whether to save
      Document1." (read-only, safe).
    - Where it is a bad idea: pressing Save / Don't save for you. Don't save loses work; Save can
      overwrite or open a file picker. If ever built: only after a yes that names the exact button and
      dialog text (confirm.py), never chosen by the LLM.
    - Never add /F (force kill): that is the path that really skips the warning.
  Settings and changes:
    - Good: a setting with no command or API (some Settings-app toggles, e.g. Bluetooth device pairing,
      parts of Focus / Do Not Disturb), and checking that a change really took (read the toggle back).
    - Bad as the default: breaks when Windows updates or the language changes; needs the window open
      and in front (steals focus, bad mid-game); takes seconds; can press the wrong thing if the layout
      moves; harder to test (test_jarvis can only fake it).
    - Rule if used: command / API / registry first; UI Automation only for a named setting with no
      other way; find controls by AutomationId from a fixed table (never by screen position, never from
      model output); read the value back and report honestly; time-limited; protected changes behind
      ACTIONS + confirm.py like every other action.
  Tools: PowerShell's built-in UIAutomationClient (.NET, no new dependency) or pywinauto (a new
  dependency: needs the user's approval). Prefer the built-in one for a few settings.
  Verdict (for the user to confirm): yes for read-only checks (which dialog is open, did the toggle
  change) and a short list of no-API settings; no for closing apps (already safe) and no as a general way
  to run commands.
